<a id="singapore-migration-guide"></a>

# Singapore: migration guide

Sources checked up to 10 Nov 2026.

How to go to Singapore from Bangladesh, visa by visa, step by step, for Bangladesh passport holders, as of November 2026. A raised number such as 3 is an official source; N3 is a news, NGO (non-governmental organisation), research or guide source, listed under "Needs an official source"; a year, like N3 (2022), marks a source older than 2025. "Need to know" marks a point no source has yet confirmed. "You'll need:" lists what to have before starting a procedure.

S$ is the Singapore dollar; Tk is the Bangladeshi taka (1 lakh is Tk 100,000). S$1 is about Tk 97: Bangladesh Bank's cross rate on 19 October 2026 was S$1 = Tk 96.59 to 96.69, and taka figures in this doc are converted at that rate unless a source gives its own <sup>[1](#o1)</sup>. A Bangladesh passport holder needs a visa, or a work pass approval, before travelling to Singapore <sup>[2](#o2)</sup>.

<a id="background"></a>

## 1 Background

<a id="in-one-minute"></a>

### 1.1 In one minute

Singapore is now Bangladesh's second destination for workers: BMET (the Bureau of Manpower, Employment and Training, the Bangladesh government body that registers and clears migrant workers) cleared 66,657 for Singapore from 1 January to 7 November 2026, behind only Saudi Arabia <sup>[3](#o3)</sup>. Almost all are men, most of them in construction and the rest in shipyards, process plants, conservancy and landscaping <sup>[4](#o4), [5](#o5)</sup>. They go on a Work Permit, Singapore's pass for unskilled and semi-skilled foreign workers, which construction firms may use to hire Bangladeshis <sup>[6](#o6), [7](#o7)</sup>. For a construction worker tested in Bangladesh, the job starts as one of the foreign-worker places an employer may fill under its quota, and only a licensed chain can turn it into a visa: the Singapore receiving agent may get an IPA (in-principle approval, the letter that lets the worker fly) only for candidates from its partner agency in Bangladesh, and that agency is attached to one of the five test centres in Bangladesh that Singapore's Building and Construction Authority (BCA) approves <sup>[7](#o7), [8 (2018)](#o8), [9](#o9), [10](#o10)</sup>. Bangladesh's state minister for expatriates' welfare said in September 2026 that six companies in Singapore effectively monopolise recruitment <sup>[N1](#n1)</sup>. The government cap is Tk 2,62,270, but first-time workers are asked for S$9,000 to 13,000 (about Tk 8.7 to 12.6 lakh) and some pay up to Tk 16 lakh, against an average construction basic salary of about S$820 (about Tk 80,000) a month <sup>[11 (2019)](#o11), [N2](#n2), [N1](#n1), [N3](#n3), [1](#o1)</sup>. The Work Permit ties the worker to one employer, who decides whether to renew it, so some workers pay "renewal money" to keep the job, and indebted workers rarely complain <sup>[6](#o6), [12](#o12), [N4 (2024)](#n4), [N5 (2017)](#n5), [5](#o5)</sup>. From January 2028 Singapore ends the skills test in Bangladesh and construction workers will be tested in Singapore after arrival, which Bangladesh's High Commission expects to cut the costs driven by local brokers and syndicates <sup>[13](#o13), [5](#o5)</sup>.

<a id="is-it-open-now-and-who-can-go"></a>

### 1.2 Is it open now, and who can go?

Singapore is open and taking Bangladeshis at record levels: BMET cleared 70,832 in 2025, the most in its yearly figures since 2004, and 66,657 from 1 January to 7 November 2026, more than one in ten of all Bangladeshis cleared for work abroad, making it the second destination after Saudi Arabia <sup>[14](#o14), [15](#o15), [3](#o3)</sup>. Nearly all are men, and most work in construction <sup>[4](#o4), [5](#o5)</sup>.

<a id="status"></a>

#### 1.2.1 Status

Open as of 8 November 2026, and growing, while recruitment to Malaysia, Oman and Bahrain has been largely shut for years <sup>[3](#o3), [N1](#n1)</sup>. Singapore's Ministry of Manpower (MOM) counted 1,243,600 Work Permit holders in June 2026, up from 849,700 in December 2021; 490,700 of them work in the CMP sectors (construction, marine shipyard and process, meaning petrochemical and refinery plants), where most Bangladeshis work <sup>[16](#o16), [5](#o5)</sup>. Singapore's construction sector grew 11.8% year on year in the first quarter of 2026 <sup>[N1](#n1)</sup>. Prime Minister Lawrence Wong said on 23 August 2026 that Singapore would keep taking foreign workers but in a "careful and controlled way" <sup>[N1](#n1)</sup>.

Unpaid salary is rising: the Migrant Workers' Centre (MWC, set up by Singapore's National Trades Union Congress and National Employers Federation to help migrant workers) said the number of workers filing salary claims in the first ten months of 2026 had more than doubled from the same period of 2024 and 2025 <sup>[N6](#n6), [N7](#n7)</sup>. More than 230 workers, mostly Bangladeshi, of Process Maintenance Solutions and Jurong Technical and Construction went unpaid from August 2026 and had their Work Permits cancelled from late September; MOM is investigating both companies <sup>[N8](#n8)</sup>. Their directors had earlier taken over two firms owned by a man arrested in July 2026 on suspicion of employment offences, a case in which MOM suspects a criminal syndicate may be involved <sup>[N8](#n8)</sup>. Earlier in 2026 two other companies went bankrupt, and about 250 of their Bangladeshi workers were re-employed with MOM's help <sup>[5](#o5)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Who is the man arrested in July 2026, and were the two companies that went bankrupt that month among his firms?

<a id="the-channels"></a>

#### 1.2.2 The channels

- *Work Permit, construction, tested in Bangladesh.* The main route: the worker passes the SEC(K) (Skills Evaluation Certificate (Knowledge)) trade test at a BCA-approved Overseas Testing Centre (OTC) before he flies (see [Step 3: The skills test](#step-3-the-skills-test): The skills test) <sup>[13](#o13), [10](#o10)</sup>.
- *Work Permit, construction, untested.* A worker can also go without the test, be trained under the employer's arrangement and take it in Singapore; this route costs the worker more, and some who fail are sent back <sup>[N9 (2024)](#n9)</sup>. BCA's rule is that every new construction Work Permit holder passes the SEC(K) before starting work, but MOM does issue permits without the certificate, levied at S$900 a month, the same rate as a basic-skilled Bangladeshi <sup>[13](#o13), [7](#o7)</sup>.
- *Work Permit, marine shipyard and process.* The BCA test is for construction workers <sup>[13](#o13)</sup>. Workers here take longest to earn back their fees: 8.6 months' salary in process plants and 7.2 in shipyards, against 2.9 in construction (see ["What you pay, and what you earn and keep"](#what-you-pay-and-what-you-earn-and-keep)) <sup>[N3](#n3)</sup>.
- *Work Permit, services and manufacturing.* Open to Bangladeshis only for the occupations on MOM's Non-Traditional Sources (NTS) Occupation List, such as cooks, food-processing workers, welders and heavy-vehicle drivers, at a fixed monthly salary of at least S$2,000 <sup>[17](#o17), [18](#o18)</sup>.
- *Returnees.* A former secretary general of the Singapore sending agencies' association said in 2026 that a significant share of departures are workers going back on new visas, not first-timers <sup>[N1](#n1)</sup>.

For construction workers tested in Bangladesh, only listed agencies can send: Bangladesh lets a recruiting agency send them only as a "sending organisation" (SO), attached to a test centre and contracted to a Singapore receiving agent <sup>[8 (2018)](#o8), [9](#o9)</sup>. Each Singapore SO deposits Tk 30 lakh with BMET (who holds these places: see [Who controls it](#who-controls-it)) <sup>[N9 (2024)](#n9), [N10 (2022)](#n10)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Which agencies are Singapore sending organisations in November 2026, and with which test centre and Singapore receiving agent?
> - The 2018 rules also let "other Recruitment Licence holders" put workers forward for Singapore: may any licensed agency send shipyard, process or services workers, who take no BCA test <sup>[8 (2018)](#o8)</sup>?
> - Until testing moves to Singapore in 2028, how does a new Bangladeshi worker go to a construction job untested, and what does it cost?
> - What share of 2025-26 clearances were construction, shipyard, process and services workers?

<a id="who-qualifies"></a>

#### 1.2.3 Who qualifies

- *Sex.* Men: 126 women were cleared for Singapore in 2025, of 70,832 <sup>[4](#o4), [14](#o14)</sup>.
- *Age.* MOM takes construction Work Permit applicants aged 18 to 62, who may work until 64 <sup>[7](#o7)</sup>.
- *Nationality.* Bangladesh is one of MOM's "non-traditional sources" from which construction firms may hire <sup>[7](#o7)</sup>.
- *Skill.* A new construction worker must pass both the written and practical parts of the SEC(K) in one trade, such as formwork, steel reinforcement, plumbing, electrical wiring, welding, tiling or waterproofing <sup>[13](#o13), [19](#o19)</sup>. Recruiters said in 2026 that employers increasingly prefer workers with several certificates <sup>[N1](#n1)</sup>.

<a id="where-the-workers-come-from"></a>

#### 1.2.4 Where the workers come from

In 2025 Tangail sent 10,423 of the 70,832 workers cleared for Singapore, about one in seven, followed by Munshiganj (5,183), Cumilla (3,599), Meherpur (3,289), Gazipur (2,723), Pabna, Jamalpur, Dhaka, Chandpur and Kushtia; all four Sylhet districts together sent 324 <sup>[20](#o20)</sup>. Tangail led again in 2026, with 10,137 by 7 November <sup>[21](#o21)</sup>.

<a id="the-size-of-the-flow"></a>

#### 1.2.5 The size of the flow

About 135,000 Bangladeshis work in Singapore by BMET's count, mostly in construction and also in shipyards, process plants, conservancy (town-council cleaning) and landscaping; other estimates run to 200,000 <sup>[5](#o5), [N1](#n1)</sup>. MOM does not publish its data by nationality <sup>[N11](#n11)</sup>. Labour migration to Singapore began in 1979 with 110 workers, and 913,159 went from 2004 to May 2025, 6.54% of all Bangladeshi overseas employment <sup>[N12 (2022)](#n12), [15](#o15)</sup>. By BMET's online clearance reports, Singapore was the fourth destination in 2024, after Saudi Arabia, Malaysia and Qatar, and the sixth in 2023 <sup>[22](#o22), [23](#o23)</sup>. Workers who went to Singapore, by year:
- 2014: 54,750 <sup>[15](#o15)</sup>
- 2015: 55,523 <sup>[15](#o15)</sup>
- 2016: 54,730 <sup>[15](#o15)</sup>
- 2017: 40,401 <sup>[15](#o15)</sup>
- 2018: 41,393 <sup>[15](#o15)</sup>
- 2019: 49,829 <sup>[15](#o15)</sup>
- 2020: 10,085 <sup>[15](#o15)</sup>
- 2021: 27,875 <sup>[15](#o15)</sup>
- 2022: 64,383 <sup>[15](#o15)</sup>
- 2023: 53,265 <sup>[15](#o15)</sup>
- 2024: 56,878 <sup>[15](#o15)</sup>
- 2025: 70,832 <sup>[14](#o14)</sup>
- 2026, to 7 November: 66,657 <sup>[3](#o3)</sup>

<a id="where-they-live"></a>

#### 1.2.6 Where they live

About 1,600 dormitories held about 467,000 beds for migrant workers in June 2026, and MOM is studying a temporary relaxation of the occupancy limits while new dormitories are built <sup>[24](#o24)</sup>. On Sundays Bangladeshi workers crowd the field in front of the Mustafa Centre in Little India <sup>[N13 (2020)](#n13)</sup>.

<a id="what-has-closed-it-before"></a>

#### 1.2.7 What has closed it before

- *2020, COVID-19 in the dormitories.* Singapore's outbreak was mostly in worker dormitories: 54,505 of the 58,320 people who tested positive by PCR (swab) test to 13 December 2020 were dormitory residents, and with blood tests 47% of the 323,000 residents had been infected <sup>[25 (2020)](#o25)</sup>. Among dormitory residents there were 25 COVID-related intensive-care admissions and two COVID deaths <sup>[25 (2020)](#o25)</sup>. All dormitories were isolated by mid-April 2020 <sup>[25 (2020)](#o25)</sup>. Bangladeshis were the most-infected group early on, 363 of the first 1,910 cases <sup>[N13 (2020)](#n13)</sup>. Departures fell from 49,829 in 2019 to 10,085 in 2020 <sup>[15](#o15)</sup>.
- *May 2021, entry ban.* From 1 May 2021 Singapore barred entry to all long-term pass holders and visitors who had been in Bangladesh in the previous 14 days, even those already approved; the ban still stood in October 2021 <sup>[26 (2021)](#o26), [27 (2021)](#o27)</sup>.
- *Late 2021, reopening.* From 1 October 2021 to 31 March 2022 MOM let construction and process firms hire workers without the minimum skill requirement, and departures reached a then-record 64,383 in 2022 <sup>[N12 (2022)](#n12), [15](#o15)</sup>.

<a id="where-it-may-grow-and-where-it-may-tighten"></a>

#### 1.2.8 Where it may grow, and where it may tighten

- *January 2028: the test moves to Singapore.* Pre-entry SEC(K) testing ends for all source countries in January 2028 (January 2027 for China and Thailand); a new construction worker must then pass it in Singapore within his first year to have his permit renewed <sup>[13](#o13)</sup>. Bangladesh's High Commission expects this to cut the costs driven by local brokers and syndicates <sup>[5](#o5)</sup>.
- *September 2026: more services jobs.* MOM added eight occupations in food services, social services and air transport to the NTS Occupation List <sup>[18](#o18)</sup>.
- *2028: dearer basic-skilled shipyard and process workers.* The monthly levy employers pay for a basic-skilled worker rises by S$100 in shipyards and S$150 in process plants <sup>[18](#o18)</sup>.
- *Competition from India and Myanmar.* In 2024 Singapore recruiters said employers were turning to workers from other countries, naming Myanmar and India, and blamed false insurance claims and complaints filed against employers <sup>[N9 (2024)](#n9)</sup>. In construction, Bangladeshis' average basic salary (S$820) is close to Myanmar workers' (S$829) and below Indians' (S$911) <sup>[N3](#n3)</sup>.
- *Skills.* Migration researcher Mohammad Jalal Uddin Sikder of North South University said in September 2026 that demand may shift from low- and semi-skilled to skilled and highly skilled workers <sup>[N1](#n1)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Is there a Bangladesh-Singapore memorandum of understanding (MoU) on labour, or one under negotiation?

<a id="how-a-job-reaches-you-and-who-takes-a-cut"></a>

### 1.3 How a job reaches you, and who takes a cut

A Singapore job starts as a Work Permit place an employer can fill under its quota (in construction, five Work Permit holders for each local employee paid at least the Local Qualifying Salary, the pay a local needs to count towards the quota), and reaches a Bangladeshi village as a job sold for lakhs of taka <sup>[7](#o7), [N2](#n2)</sup>. The choke point is the licensed chain between the two: only a test centre approved by BCA can certify a construction worker, only the agencies attached to it can send him, and only their partner agents in Singapore can get him an IPA <sup>[10](#o10), [9](#o9), [8 (2018)](#o8)</sup>.

<a id="the-chain-by-rule"></a>

#### 1.3.1 The chain, by rule

Under the Ministry of Expatriates' Welfare's guidelines of 1 October 2018:
1. BCA approves a Singapore construction or testing company to run a test centre in Bangladesh; its Bangladeshi partner holds the Ministry's NOC (no-objection certificate) and runs the centre <sup>[8 (2018)](#o8)</sup>.
2. The Bangladeshi partner and the agencies attached to it are the sending organisations (SOs); each SO has a contract with a Singapore receiving agent (RA) <sup>[8 (2018)](#o8)</sup>.
3. The SO sends the bio-data of workers who passed the test only to its RA, and the RA may get IPAs only for workers from its SO; the Ministry's SO enlistments of December 2025 repeat this condition <sup>[8 (2018)](#o8), [9](#o9)</sup>.
4. Every IPA had to be entered on the website bshrs.com.bd before BMET would issue the worker's clearance card <sup>[8 (2018)](#o8)</sup>.
5. Trading visas or IPAs is banned <sup>[8 (2018)](#o8)</sup>.

Each centre's NOC lasts one year, and the holder must apply to renew it by 31 December; by rule a five-member committee first inspects the centre and checks its BCA approval <sup>[8 (2018)](#o8), [N14 (2023)](#n14)</sup>. In December 2025, for example, the Ministry enlisted S. Nabiha Overseas (recruiting licence RL-2638) as an SO through the test centre Progressive Dhaka Technology Ltd, with Hundred Miles Human Resources Pte Ltd as its Singapore receiving agent <sup>[9](#o9)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - bshrs.com.bd no longer resolves: where must an IPA be registered now before BMET clearance, and who runs Bangladesh Singapore Harmonized Resource System Ltd?

<a id="the-test-centres"></a>

#### 1.3.2 The test centres

BCA's list of 25 September 2025 has five approved test centres in Bangladesh, four in Ashulia, Savar and Turag near Dhaka and one in Gazipur, each run by a Singapore firm: Fonda Global Engineering, Santarli Construction, Singapore Engineering & Construction, Setsco Services and Lian Beng Construction (names, addresses and phones in Work Permit, [Step 3: The skills test](#step-3-the-skills-test)) <sup>[10](#o10)</sup>.

BCA also approves these centres to run training courses <sup>[10](#o10)</sup>. Only firms on BCA's Contractors Registration System can register a worker for a test, and they should register only as many as their quota allows <sup>[13](#o13)</sup>. The employer, not the worker, pays the test fee, and Bangladesh's rules bar any extra collection (amounts in [Step 3: The skills test](#step-3-the-skills-test) of the Work Permit) <sup>[13](#o13), [8 (2018)](#o8)</sup>. The Ministry's 2018 guidelines went to eight centres, among them WellTech, CT Test Centre and Progressive Test Centre in Ashulia, none of which is on BCA's 2025 list <sup>[8 (2018)](#o8), [10](#o10)</sup>. In 2024 a centre could test 70 workers a month, against about 200 just after COVID <sup>[N9 (2024)](#n9)</sup>.

<a id="who-controls-it"></a>

#### 1.3.3 Who controls it

- In February 2022 a former senior official of BAIRA (the Bangladesh Association of International Recruiting Agencies) said 13 agencies held a syndicate monopoly and took Tk 6 to 8 lakh per worker, and asked that every licensed agency be allowed to send <sup>[N12 (2022)](#n12)</sup>.
- By April 2024 the authorities of both countries had allowed 10 agencies to set up training centres for Singapore, each with a Tk 30 lakh deposit at BMET <sup>[N9 (2024)](#n9)</sup>.
- In September 2026 State Minister for Expatriates' Welfare Nurul Haque Nur said six companies in Singapore effectively monopolise recruitment and that workers are reportedly charged up to Tk 16 lakh where the cost should be about Tk 4 to 5 lakh; he said the government would summon the agencies and had discussed costs with BCA <sup>[N1](#n1)</sup>.
- On 30 September 2026 the Ministry's secretary told workers at the High Commission that complaints against the "OTC-centred syndicate" of local agencies would be investigated <sup>[5](#o5)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Which six companies in Singapore did the State Minister say monopolise recruitment, and are they the Singapore firms behind the five BCA test centres?
> - Did the government summon the agencies after September 2026, and with what result?

<a id="down-the-chain"></a>

#### 1.3.4 Down the chain

Below the licensed chain sit private training centres, many advertising on Facebook with promises such as "train and get a Singapore job paying Tk 1 lakh" <sup>[N15 (2021)](#n15)</sup>. Some centres took Tk 38,000 to 40,000 at admission against a package of about Tk 6 lakh, kept the worker's passport, and demanded Tk 1.5 to 2 lakh more after the training or test; one centre's website now lists one-month courses at Tk 12,000 <sup>[N15 (2021)](#n15), [N16](#n16)</sup>. BMET's director of training said in 2021 that its 60 training centres train for a nominal fee, but workers go to private ones for lack of knowledge <sup>[N15 (2021)](#n15)</sup>. The "agent" a worker pays may be a friend, a contact at the training centre or an employee of the future employer <sup>[N17 (2024)](#n17)</sup>. From November 2025 TWC2 (Transient Workers Count Too, a Singapore migrant-worker charity) registered 114 unpaid Bangladeshi first-timers from three connected companies; most believed the bulk of what they paid went to their Singapore bosses, and one was told by his agent that his Tk 10 lakh had been handed to a boss <sup>[N2](#n2)</sup>. Those three companies had no projects of their own and hired the men out to other firms as "supply workers" <sup>[N2](#n2)</sup>. For later jobs about half of workers paid part or all of the fee to someone in Singapore, sometimes the employer himself <sup>[N5 (2017)](#n5)</sup>. Workers said in 2021 that Bangladeshis in Singapore trade visas, selling each for lakhs of taka <sup>[N15 (2021)](#n15)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - In 2026, how is a Tk 10 to 16 lakh Singapore payment split between the training centre, the SO, the Singapore receiving agent, the employer and the broker?
> - What does an IPA sell for in Dhaka in 2026?

<a id="kickbacks-and-renewal-money"></a>

#### 1.3.5 Kickbacks and renewal money

A kickback is money an employer or his managers demand from a worker, with the threat of cancelling or not renewing his Work Permit <sup>[N11](#n11)</sup>. An employer may not take any money from anyone in connection with a worker's employment <sup>[6](#o6)</sup>. Collecting kickbacks is a crime under Singapore's Employment of Foreign Manpower Act: up to two years' jail, a fine of up to S$30,000, or both <sup>[28](#o28), [N11](#n11)</sup>. Two recent convictions, both at estate-cleaning firms, used fellow workers or agents as collectors:
- *Lian Cheng Contracting.* Operations manager Derrick Ho was jailed 24 weeks in 2024 for taking S$1,500 for each one-year renewal (S$3,000 for two years) from 57 workers, nearly S$400,000 in all; the four worker-supervisors who collected for him were warned and barred from Singapore <sup>[N4 (2024)](#n4)</sup>. One worker paid about S$15,000 over 12 years and did not see his family in that time <sup>[N4 (2024)](#n4)</sup>.
- *Weishen Industrial Services.* Loo Kim Huat, a director of its managing company WIS Holdings, was fined S$90,000, plus a S$42,000 penalty order, in July 2025 for collecting S$112,400 from 18 workers, S$900 to 7,000 each, to renew their passes, together with a former site manager, two conservancy workers and a Bangladesh-based agent; he repaid S$83,050 to the workers <sup>[28](#o28)</sup>.

Kickbacks were commonplace ten years ago and are fewer now, but "churn" can bring employers the same money: the employer declines to renew and hires a new worker, whose agent fee, paid at home, is shared with the employer <sup>[N11](#n11), [N4 (2024)](#n4)</sup>. MOM helps a worker who reports a kickback to change employer (see [Changing employer or leaving](#changing-employer-or-leaving)) <sup>[N11](#n11)</sup>.

<a id="the-weltec-case"></a>

#### 1.3.6 The Weltec case

In 2018 BCA cancelled the training and testing licence of The Gazipur Air International and its Singapore partner, Weltec Construction, which ran the Weltec test centre in Ashulia <sup>[N14 (2023)](#n14), [N10 (2022)](#n10)</sup>. The daily Ittefaq reported in March 2023 that the agency kept renewing its Ministry licence with fake BCA approval certificates until 2022 and took clearance for about 60,000 workers through three other agencies, Al Jannat Overseas, RX Care International and Weltec Employment Services; a BMET inquiry of 29 December 2022 found the centre was not on BCA's list, but no action had been taken <sup>[N14 (2023)](#n14), [N10 (2022)](#n10)</sup>. The Gazipur Air International's owner declined to comment in November 2022 <sup>[N10 (2022)](#n10)</sup>. Ittefaq also reported in November 2022 that BMET had show-caused Oasis Overseas Services, which took clearance for 3,037 Singapore workers after being dropped from the SO list <sup>[N10 (2022)](#n10)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Were the four Weltec agencies or Oasis Overseas punished after 2023?
> - Were the workers sent through the Weltec agencies able to renew their permits?

<a id="what-you-pay-and-what-you-earn-and-keep"></a>

### 1.4 What you pay, and what you earn and keep

The government cap for Singapore is Tk 2,62,270; on the Singapore side an employment agency may take at most one month's salary for each year of service and two months' in all, and the employer pays the test and the security bond (details under Who pays what in the Work Permit section) <sup>[11 (2019)](#o11), [N12 (2022)](#n12), [29 (2024)](#o29), [13](#o13), [30](#o30)</sup>. First-time workers are asked for S$9,000 to 13,000 (about Tk 8.7 to 12.6 lakh), and some pay up to Tk 16 lakh <sup>[N2](#n2), [N1](#n1), [1](#o1)</sup>. S$9,000 to 13,000 is 11 to 16 months of an average Bangladeshi construction worker's basic salary <sup>[N2](#n2), [N3](#n3)</sup>.

<a id="what-people-really-pay"></a>

#### 1.4.1 What people really pay

- *2026.* Up to Tk 16 lakh; over Tk 14 lakh for new workers, said a Tangail man who paid Tk 2 lakh in 1996 <sup>[N1](#n1)</sup>.
- *2025-26.* S$9,000 to 13,000 asked of nearly all first-time Bangladeshis, including basic skills training for construction workers <sup>[N2](#n2)</sup>.
- *2026 survey of Bangladeshi Work Permit holders.* 58% paid for their current job, S$3,259 on average, an average lowered by long-serving workers who pay little <sup>[N3](#n3)</sup>.
- *2024.* Tk 8 to 10 lakh; the trade said Tk 3 to 4 lakh would be fair for a certified worker, and the secretary general of the Singapore sending agencies' association said no more than Tk 4 lakh <sup>[N9 (2024)](#n9)</sup>.
- *By sector, 2026.* Across Bangladeshi, Indian and Myanmar workers who paid for their current job: S$2,499 on average in construction, S$4,096 in marine shipyard, S$6,665 in process, S$5,015 in manufacturing and S$4,672 in services <sup>[N3](#n3)</sup>.
- *Shipyards, 2016-19.* First-time workers paid S$7,000 to 9,000; one paid S$8,000 to a Bangladeshi agency in 2016, then S$2,000 more for training in Singapore <sup>[N11](#n11), [N18 (2020)](#n18)</sup>.
- *2015-18.* Tk 5,74,241 on average, the costliest destination in the Bangladesh Bureau of Statistics' Cost of Migration Survey 2020 <sup>[N19 (2020)](#n19)</sup>.
- *2015.* S$15,555 on average for a first job and S$4,299 for a later one; first-time construction workers needed 35 months to repay <sup>[N5 (2017)](#n5), [N18 (2020)](#n18)</sup>.

MOM's own survey puts typical 2024 fees for first-time workers who paid far lower (construction S$1,000 to 7,800; shipyard S$1,200 to 4,800; process S$600 to 7,200), and MOM does not publish them by nationality <sup>[N11](#n11)</sup>. Workers going back for a second or later job pay less, and some eventually pay nothing <sup>[N3](#n3)</sup>. Hiring workers already in Singapore often involves no fee, because employers interview them directly <sup>[N4 (2024)](#n4)</sup>.

<a id="how-they-pay"></a>

#### 1.4.2 How they pay

Most of the 114 workers described under Down the chain had paid in taka to an agent in Bangladesh; a few paid the Singapore boss directly, one into his bank account in instalments <sup>[N2](#n2)</sup>. Training centres have demanded more after the test without giving receipts, and one worker believes his centre failed him on purpose because it had no visa for him yet <sup>[N15 (2021)](#n15)</sup>. In the 2020 survey of all destinations, 78% of migration costs were borrowed from relatives, NGOs, moneylenders and microcredit lenders <sup>[N19 (2020)](#n19)</sup>. One worker who lost his job in 2026 still owed a S$6,000 loan for his trip <sup>[N8](#n8)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - In 2026, what are the payment stages for a Singapore construction job (training-centre admission, after the test, on the IPA, at BMET clearance), and how much is paid at each?

<a id="what-the-job-pays"></a>

#### 1.4.3 What the job pays

- *Construction.* Bangladeshis' basic salary averages S$820 a month (2026); construction pay rises from about S$690 a month for newcomers to S$1,075 after ten years <sup>[N3](#n3)</sup>.
- *Shipyards.* The lowest: S$567 a month on average, and S$705 after ten years <sup>[N3](#n3)</sup>. Deductions often exceed allowances: in 37 shipyard IPAs TWC2 examined, deductions averaged S$79 a month against S$2 of allowances, leaving the fixed salary S$76 below the basic <sup>[N20](#n20)</sup>.
- *Process.* S$771 a month on average <sup>[N3](#n3)</sup>.
- *All sectors, by experience.* Bangladeshi newcomers (under 2 years) averaged S$526 a month in 2026, and those with more than 10 years S$1,044 <sup>[N3](#n3)</sup>.

Overtime comes on top; in 2015-18 Singapore was the best-paid destination, at Tk 46,895 a month on average <sup>[N3](#n3), [N19 (2020)](#n19)</sup>. The salary the employer must pay is the one on the IPA (basic plus fixed allowances minus monthly deductions), and the courts enforce it unless it is shown that something else was genuinely agreed <sup>[N3](#n3), [N21](#n21)</sup>. About one in ten Work Permit holders told MOM's 2024 survey they had not received an IPA for their current job, many have no written contract, and some employers agree a salary with the worker but declare a lower one to MOM <sup>[N21](#n21), [N3](#n3)</sup>. About two in three workers TWC2 surveyed in 2026 could not correctly state the salary components on their own IPA <sup>[N3](#n3)</sup>.

<a id="how-long-the-debt-takes"></a>

#### 1.4.4 How long the debt takes

At 2026 averages a worker needs about 2.9 months of salary to recover what he paid for his current job in construction, 7.2 months in shipyards and 8.6 months in process plants, but that sample includes many long-serving workers who paid little <sup>[N3](#n3)</sup>. A first-timer who pays S$10,000 on an S$820 basic needs about a year of basic pay <sup>[N2](#n2), [N3](#n3)</sup>. In 2011, when the average fee was S$7,256, Bangladeshis needed 17.5 months to recover it; workers said in 2021 that it took at least four to five years <sup>[N5 (2017)](#n5), [N15 (2021)](#n15)</sup>. Workers who have not recovered their costs feel unable to complain about unpaid salary, unsafe work or housing, because losing the job means going home and paying an agent again <sup>[N5 (2017)](#n5)</sup>.

<a id="remittance-from-singapore"></a>

#### 1.4.5 Remittance from Singapore

Bangladesh Bank recorded US$385 million of remittance from Singapore in 2021-22 and US$423 million in 2022-23 <sup>[31](#o31)</sup>. The expatriates' welfare minister told Parliament in September 2026 that it reached US$1.49 billion in 2025-26 <sup>[N22](#n22), [N1](#n1)</sup>; Bangladesh Bank's own figures for July to September 2026 show US$87.71 million, the eighth-largest source <sup>[32](#o32)</sup>. The legal channels are under Sending money home.

> **Need to know**
> - Which figure is right for remittance from Singapore: about US$1.5 billion a year, as the minister said, or under US$100 million a quarter, as Bangladesh Bank's July-September 2026 figures show?

<a id="what-ties-you-to-the-employer"></a>

### 1.5 What ties you to the employer

You are tied to your employer for everything: the Work Permit lets you work only for that employer and in that occupation and sector, and it is the employer who renews, cancels and pays for your ticket home <sup>[6](#o6), [12](#o12)</sup>.

- **Entering.** The IPA names one company and one occupation, and the employer cannot move you to another <sup>[5](#o5)</sup>.
- **Renewing.** The employer decides, and has priority to renew; from 2028 a new construction worker must also pass the SEC(K) in Singapore to be renewed <sup>[12](#o12), [13](#o13)</sup>. "Renewal money" is a crime (see [Kickbacks and renewal money](#kickbacks-and-renewal-money)) <sup>[28](#o28)</sup>.
- **Quitting.** The employer cancels the permit within a week of your last day of notice, buys a one-way ticket for a flight within 14 days, and gives you a Special Pass to stay until then; you cannot work while you wait <sup>[6](#o6), [33](#o33)</sup>.
- **Changing employer.** A new construction employer can hire you only with your current employer's consent, given within 7 days, unless your permit is 40 to 21 days from expiry <sup>[12](#o12)</sup>. A shipyard worker cannot move to another sector inside Singapore, and TWC2 says Seatrium, the largest shipyard operator, makes it hard to move between its contractors <sup>[N20](#n20)</sup>. MOM helps a worker who reports a kickback to change employer, and MWC is helping the unpaid workers of November 2026 apply to change employer, both those hired at the job fair and those who found new jobs on their own (see [Changing employer or leaving](#changing-employer-or-leaving)) <sup>[N11](#n11), [N8](#n8)</sup>.
- **Leaving.** The employer must pay your ticket home whatever the reason the job ended <sup>[34 (2022)](#o34)</sup>.

*Rules on your life.* You must live where the employer says, may not run a business, and may not marry a Singapore citizen or permanent resident without MOM's approval <sup>[6](#o6)</sup>. The employer pays a S$5,000 security bond for you and may not charge it to you; if the employer does not pay your salary, the bond can pay your arrears and flight home, but only up to about S$5,000 a worker <sup>[30](#o30), [N2](#n2)</sup>.

*An expired or cancelled permit.* Staying on is overstaying, which can bring jail and caning, then deportation and a ban (penalties under What can get you jailed or deported, "Overstaying") <sup>[35](#o35), [36](#o36)</sup>. A Bangladeshi whose Special Pass expired in January 2021, and who stayed on working as a rice packer, was arrested in April 2021, jailed a month and given three strokes of the cane; in May 2026 the Bangladeshi work-pass holder who employed him was jailed 12 months <sup>[35](#o35)</sup>.

<a id="what-goes-wrong-most-and-what-people-wrongly-believe"></a>

### 1.6 What goes wrong most, and what people wrongly believe

In 2026 salary claims more than doubled, and whole companies of mostly Bangladeshi workers went unpaid <sup>[N7](#n7), [N8](#n8)</sup>. Injuries and forced repatriation are the other harms below; low declared salaries and kickbacks are under What the job pays and Kickbacks and renewal money <sup>[37](#o37), [N23 (2014)](#n23), [N21](#n21), [N4 (2024)](#n4)</sup>.

*What goes wrong.*
- *Unpaid salary.* From November 2025 TWC2 registered 114 unpaid Bangladeshis from three connected companies, all of whom had been asked to pay S$9,000 to 13,000 for the job <sup>[N2](#n2)</sup>. When workers told MOM they had paid for their jobs in Bangladesh or in cash, they were told MOM could do nothing <sup>[N2](#n2)</sup>.
- *Injury and death.* 36 people died at work in Singapore in 2025, 13 of them in construction; across all sectors, vehicle accidents, falls from height and collapsing structures caused 78% of the deaths <sup>[37](#o37)</sup>. Workers ride to sites in the backs of lorries: in April 2021 a Bangladeshi died when a lorry carrying 17 workers was hit on the Pan-Island Expressway, and in 2016 a reversing lorry killed two Bangladeshis resting after lunch <sup>[N24 (2021)](#n24), [N25 (2016)](#n25)</sup>. There were 28,895 work injury claims in 2025; how to claim is under "If you are injured at work" <sup>[37](#o37)</sup>.
- *Forced repatriation.* Employers and agents have used "repatriation agents" to march a complaining worker to the airport; in a 2014 case an officer at the airport checkpoint told the worker he could simply walk out if he did not want to board <sup>[N23 (2014)](#n23)</sup>. In 2013 MOM stepped in on 23 departures flagged at the airport because the worker had unsettled employment matters <sup>[N23 (2014)](#n23)</sup>.
- *Few complain.* Workers told the Bangladesh High Commission at meetings from May to July 2026 about high costs and low pay, but none filed a written complaint with evidence <sup>[5](#o5)</sup>. BMET's director general said in 2020 that no worker questioned at the airport admitted what he paid <sup>[N19 (2020)](#n19)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How many Bangladeshi workers die in Singapore each year, and of what causes?

*"The training centre guarantees a job."* The centre is not the employer: the job comes with the IPA, which the employer's Singapore agent obtains, and centres have taken workers' money and passports, demanded more after the test, and sent them to supply companies (to check an offer, see [Step 4: Get a real job offer](#step-4-get-a-real-job-offer): Get a real job offer) <sup>[8 (2018)](#o8), [N15 (2021)](#n15)</sup>.

*"Singapore pays Tk 1 lakh a month."* Training-centre adverts say so <sup>[N15 (2021)](#n15)</sup>. A Bangladeshi construction worker's basic salary averages about S$820 (about Tk 80,000), and a shipyard worker's monthly salary about S$567 (about Tk 55,000) <sup>[N3](#n3), [1](#o1)</sup>.

*"Go on a visit visa and find work there."* A visitor may not do any work, paid or unpaid, without a work pass <sup>[38](#o38)</sup>. Overstaying can bring jail and caning <sup>[35](#o35)</sup>.

*"Once in Singapore you can work anywhere."* A Work Permit holder may work only for his employer, in the occupation on his permit <sup>[6](#o6)</sup>. In 2020 ICA (the Immigration and Checkpoints Authority, Singapore's immigration department) arrested four Bangladeshis suspected of overstaying who were found doing cleaning work in private homes <sup>[39 (2020)](#o39)</sup>.

*"The government rate is Tk 2,62,270."* That is the cap, set in 2017, and workers who went for it are hard to find (what people pay: see [What people really pay](#what-people-really-pay)) <sup>[11 (2019)](#o11), [N12 (2022)](#n12), [N15 (2021)](#n15)</sup>.

*How the irregular routes end.*
- *Overstaying and illegal work.* Jail, caning, deportation and a ban; the employer can be jailed too <sup>[35](#o35)</sup>.
- *A test centre without BCA approval.* Ittefaq reported in 2023 that about 60,000 workers were sent through the Weltec centre's agencies in the five years after BCA cancelled its licence (see [The Weltec case](#the-weltec-case)) <sup>[N14 (2023)](#n14)</sup>.
- *Extremist views, including online.* In late 2015 Singapore arrested 27 Bangladeshi construction workers for radical religious activity, cancelled their passes and sent 26 home <sup>[40 (2016)](#o40)</sup>. In July 2026 it cancelled the permits of two Bangladeshis for extremist posts on social media, though it found no attack plans, and sent them home, where they were detained at Dhaka airport <sup>[N26](#n26)</sup>.

<a id="visas-not-open-to-bangladeshis"></a>

## 2 Visas not open to Bangladeshis

These schemes exist but are closed to Bangladesh passport holders, or open only in narrow cases <sup>[41](#o41), [42](#o42), [43 (2024)](#o43)</sup>.

- **Visa-free entry:** a Bangladeshi with an ordinary passport needs a visa before travelling; only diplomatic, official and service passports are exempt <sup>[2](#o2), [41](#o41)</sup>. Bangladesh is on the list of countries whose citizens must apply <sup>[44](#o44)</sup>.
- **Visa on arrival:** none. A Bangladeshi must apply for a Singapore visa before arrival, and ICA takes no visa applications at its immigration counters <sup>[41](#o41), [45](#o45)</sup>.
- **96-hour Visa Free Transit Facility (VFTF):** a visa-free stopover of up to 96 hours, for nationals of the CIS (Commonwealth of Independent States: Armenia, Azerbaijan, Belarus, Kazakhstan, Kyrgyzstan, Moldova, Russia, Tajikistan, Uzbekistan), Georgia, India, Turkmenistan and Ukraine, and certain Chinese travel-document holders; Bangladesh is not on the list <sup>[42](#o42), [45](#o45)</sup>. A Bangladeshi who leaves the airport during a stopover needs a visa (see [Transit](#transit)) <sup>[46](#o46), [N27](#n27)</sup>.
- **Work Holiday Pass under the Work Holiday Programme:** up to 6 months' work and holiday, only for people aged 18 to 25 who study at, or graduated from, a university in Australia, France, Germany, Hong Kong, Japan, the Netherlands, New Zealand, Switzerland, the UK or the US, so someone studying in Bangladesh doesn't qualify; at most 2,000 people hold it at a time <sup>[43 (2024)](#o43)</sup>.
- **Work Holiday Pass under the Work and Holiday Visa Programmes:** Australian and New Zealand citizens only <sup>[47 (2024)](#o47)</sup>.

<a id="work-visa-work-permit-construction-marine-shipyard-process-and-other-sectors"></a>

## 3 Work visa: Work Permit (construction, marine shipyard, process and other sectors)

A Work Permit is Singapore's pass for unskilled and semi-skilled migrant workers: it ties you to one employer and one occupation, lasts up to 2 years at a time and can be renewed <sup>[6](#o6), [48](#o48)</sup>. Bangladesh is one of Singapore's "Non-Traditional Sources" (NTS) of workers, so Bangladeshis can get Work Permits in construction, marine shipyard and process work (together "CMP"), and in manufacturing and services only for the jobs on the NTS Occupation List <sup>[7](#o7), [49](#o49), [50](#o50), [51](#o51), [17](#o17)</sup>. Only the employer or a Singapore employment agency (EA) it appoints can apply; you cannot apply yourself <sup>[48](#o48)</sup>. The employer applies to MOM (the Ministry of Manpower, Singapore's labour ministry), and if MOM approves you get an IPA (In-Principle Approval) letter, which you show to the immigration officer when you arrive; the Work Permit itself is issued only after you arrive <sup>[52](#o52)</sup>. On the Bangladesh side, construction jobs run through skills-test centres approved by Singapore's Building and Construction Authority (BCA) and through recruiting agencies the Ministry of Expatriates' Welfare and Overseas Employment has enlisted as "Sending Organisations" for each centre ([Step 4: Get a real job offer](#step-4-get-a-real-job-offer)) <sup>[13](#o13), [8 (2018)](#o8)</sup>.

<a id="who-pays-what"></a>

### 3.1 Who pays what

<a id="what-the-employer-must-pay"></a>

#### 3.1.1 What the employer must pay

- **Application and issuance fees:** S$35 to apply and S$35 to issue the Work Permit, paid to MOM <sup>[52](#o52)</sup>.
- **Security bond:** S$5,000 for each non-Malaysian worker, as a bank or insurance guarantee; the employer cannot ask you to pay it <sup>[30](#o30)</sup>.
- **Levy:** a monthly charge per worker paid to MOM by GIRO (direct debit), from S$300 to S$900 depending on sector and skill ([Step 1: Check you qualify and which jobs are open](#step-1-check-you-qualify-and-which-jobs-are-open)) <sup>[48](#o48), [50](#o50), [7](#o7)</sup>. Deducting the levy or other employment costs from your salary carries a penalty of up to S$20,000 <sup>[53 (2024)](#o53)</sup>.
- **Skills test (construction):** the fee for the SEC(K) (Skills Evaluation Certificate (Knowledge), BCA's trade test; [Step 3: The skills test](#step-3-the-skills-test)), capped at S$925 before GST (Singapore's goods and services tax), is the sponsoring employer's to pay, to the test centre's Singapore office <sup>[13](#o13)</sup>.
- **Onboard programme** (the arrival stay for male CMP workers, with the medical and orientation; [Step 7: Arrival and the Onboard centre](#step-7-arrival-and-the-onboard-centre)): S$321.60 (3 days) or S$153.82 (2 days), booked and paid upfront by the employer or EA by card <sup>[54](#o54)</sup>.
- **Medical insurance:** at least S$60,000 a year of in-patient and day-surgery cover; the cost cannot be passed to you <sup>[55](#o55)</sup>. The employer may make you co-pay your medical bills only if the co-payment is at most 10% of your fixed monthly salary, for at most 6 months in every 2 years, and written into your contract with your consent <sup>[55](#o55)</sup>.
- **Primary Care Plan and treatment:** for CMP workers and anyone living in a dormitory for 7 or more, the employer buys the Primary Care Plan, which covers unlimited clinic consultations; you pay S$5 a clinic visit and S$2 a telemedicine consultation (S$5 for both from 1 April 2027) <sup>[48](#o48), [56](#o56), [57](#o57)</sup>. Otherwise the employer pays your medical treatment <sup>[6](#o6)</sup>.
- **Training and medical checks in Singapore:** borne by the employer <sup>[58 (2011)](#o58)</sup>.
- **Ticket home:** at the end of the job the employer pays your repatriation, including an air ticket with check-in luggage <sup>[6](#o6)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Who must pay the ticket from Dhaka to Singapore: is there any MOM rule, or is it left to the contract?

<a id="what-a-singapore-employment-agency-may-charge-you"></a>

#### 3.1.2 What a Singapore employment agency may charge you

A Singapore EA may take from you at most 1 month's salary for each year of the Work Permit or contract, whichever is shorter, and never more than 2 months' salary; "salary" means the one declared to MOM in the application <sup>[59](#o59)</sup>. The cap covers money a Bangladeshi agency collects and passes to the Singapore EA, but not the part the Bangladeshi agency keeps, nor costs outside Singapore such as training, medical checks and travel <sup>[58 (2011)](#o58), [59](#o59)</sup>. The EA must give you a written receipt itemising the fee and display its scale of fees <sup>[59](#o59)</sup>. Your IPA letter shows your fixed monthly salary and the fee paid to the Singapore EA <sup>[29 (2024)](#o29)</sup>.

**Refund:** if the employer fails to employ you, or ends your job within 6 months, the EA must refund at least 50% of the fees, within 7 working days of the termination or before you are sent home, whichever is earlier <sup>[59](#o59)</sup>. There is no refund if the contract was completed, expired or ended at your request, if you resign, or if you abscond <sup>[59](#o59), [60 (2024)](#o60)</sup>. If you were dismissed but told by the agency or employer to sign a resignation letter, contact MOM <sup>[60 (2024)](#o60)</sup>.

<a id="the-bangladesh-maximum"></a>

#### 3.1.3 The Bangladesh maximum

The Ministry set the total migration cost for Singapore at Tk 2,62,270 on 27 July 2017, training included: the highest of the 16 countries it covers (Saudi Arabia Tk 1,65,000, Malaysia Tk 1,60,000), covering the air fare, passport, health fees and related costs <sup>[N12 (2022)](#n12), [11 (2019)](#o11), [N28 (2018)](#n28)</sup>. In 2017 the Bangladesh mission in Singapore had proposed a ceiling built from Tk 55,000 for training and accommodation, Tk 25,000 to 30,000 for the air fare and Tk 25,000 to 30,000 for agent fees, with the BCA test fee and a company fee on top <sup>[N29 (2017)](#n29)</sup>. In August 2026 the State Minister for Foreign Affairs said the government is working to bring migration costs down to at most Tk 3 lakh <sup>[N30](#n30)</sup>. What workers really pay is in Background, "What people really pay" <sup>[N2](#n2), [N1](#n1)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Is the 2017 maximum of Tk 2,62,270 still in force for Singapore, or has a later order replaced it?

<a id="how-to-pay"></a>

#### 3.1.4 How to pay

Pay the Bangladeshi agency only through the bank, into the agency's own bank account, and keep every receipt <sup>[61](#o61)</sup>. One worker whose training centre gave no receipts had no proof of what he paid (see [Scams](#scams)) <sup>[N15 (2021)](#n15)</sup>.

<a id="step-1-check-you-qualify-and-which-jobs-are-open"></a>

### 3.2 Step 1: Check you qualify and which jobs are open

1. <a id="step-1-check-you-qualify-and-which-jobs-are-open-item-1"></a>**Age.** You must be at least 18 and at most 62 when the employer applies, and you can keep working until 64, Singapore's retirement age <sup>[7](#o7)</sup>. Since 1 July 2025 there is no maximum number of years you can work on Work Permits; before that the limit was 14 to 26 years <sup>[62](#o62)</sup>.
2. <a id="step-1-check-you-qualify-and-which-jobs-are-open-item-2"></a>**Pass length.** Each Work Permit lasts up to 2 years, ending 1 month before your passport expires or 2 months before the security bond expires <sup>[48](#o48), [52](#o52)</sup>. There is no minimum salary for a Work Permit, except on the NTS Occupation List <sup>[48](#o48), [17](#o17)</sup>. Work Permit holders cannot bring family on dependants' passes <sup>[48](#o48)</sup>.
3. <a id="step-1-check-you-qualify-and-which-jobs-are-open-item-3"></a>**Which sector.** What each sector allows Bangladeshis:
   - **Construction:** open to NTS workers; you need a BCA skills certificate ([Step 3: The skills test](#step-3-the-skills-test)) <sup>[7](#o7), [63](#o63)</sup>. The employer must be registered in BCA's Contractors Registration System (CRS) <sup>[7](#o7)</sup>.
   - **Marine shipyard:** shipyards and their contractors in shipbuilding or ship repair; you must work inside the shipyard, only in MOM's approved occupations for NTS workers <sup>[49](#o49)</sup>. Employers MOM classes as "non-sponsoring shipyards" or "common contractors" need its "prior approval" (valid 3 months) to hire NTS workers <sup>[64](#o64)</sup>.
   - **Process:** petroleum, petrochemical, specialty chemical and pharmaceutical plants <sup>[50](#o50)</sup>. Bangladeshis can only be "Process Maintenance and Construction Worker", that job with driving, or "Driver (Process)", building and maintaining plant equipment, not operating the plant <sup>[50](#o50)</sup>. The employer must be an ASPRI (Association of Process Industry) member on its PCM (process construction and maintenance) Controlled List <sup>[50](#o50)</sup>.
   - **Manufacturing and services (NTS Occupation List):** only the listed jobs, at a fixed monthly salary of at least S$2,000, and you may only do the job on your permit <sup>[51](#o51), [17](#o17)</sup>. Each firm may hire NTS-list workers up to 8% of its workforce <sup>[65](#o65)</sup>. The list, introduced in September 2023, covers <sup>[62](#o62)</sup>:
     - manufacturing workers: assemblers, machine operators, metal moulders, quality checkers, riggers, sheet metal workers, structural metal workers, welders and flame cutters <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>;
     - food processing workers, at firms licensed by the Singapore Food Agency (SFA) <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>;
     - housekeeping workers and porters in licensed hotels <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>;
     - heavy vehicle drivers (bus, lorry, trailer), only if you held a Singapore Class 4 or 5 licence on 1 September 2025 <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>;
     - food services workers: cooks, butchers and fishmongers, food and drink stall assistants, kitchen assistants and waiters, at Services firms with an SFA food shop or supermarket licence <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>;
     - babysitters and infant caregivers, childcare centre workers (educarers) and teacher aides, at licensed early childhood centres <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>;
     - cabin attendants, for airlines licensed to fly to and from Singapore <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.

     MOM added eight of these occupations, in food services, social services and air transport, in September 2026 <sup>[18](#o18)</sup>.
4. <a id="step-1-check-you-qualify-and-which-jobs-are-open-item-4"></a>**Quota and levy (the employer's costs).** The employer can hire 5 Work Permit holders for each local employee in construction and process, and 3 in marine shipyard <sup>[7](#o7), [50](#o50), [49](#o49)</sup>. The monthly levy per NTS worker is <sup>[7](#o7), [50](#o50), [49](#o49), [18](#o18)</sup>:
   - construction: S$500 higher-skilled (R1), S$900 basic-skilled (R2);
   - process: S$300 R1, S$650 R2;
   - marine shipyard: S$350 R1, S$500 R2.

   For a construction worker approved without the required certificate, the employer pays S$900, classed as "Unskilled" <sup>[7](#o7)</sup>. From 2028 the R2 levy rises to S$800 in process and S$600 in marine shipyard <sup>[50](#o50), [49](#o49), [18](#o18)</sup>. At least 10% of a firm's construction workers must be R1 before it can hire or renew R2 workers <sup>[7](#o7)</sup>.

5. <a id="step-1-check-you-qualify-and-which-jobs-are-open-item-5"></a>**Register on OEP.** OEP (the Overseas Employment Platform, BMET's online system; BMET is the Bureau of Manpower, Employment and Training) is where you apply online for training at a TTC (Technical Training Centre), the compulsory pre-departure orientation and emigration clearance <sup>[61](#o61), [66](#o66)</sup>.

   You'll need <sup>[67](#o67), [68](#o68)</sup>:
   - your NID (national ID card);
   - a mobile phone that can receive codes;
   - a bKash, Nagad or other mobile-banking account to pay the Tk 200 government fee.

   1. In Google Chrome open employee.oep.gov.bd/employee/login-with-otp (or scan the QR code on BMET's guide) and press "অনুগ্রহ করে এখানে ক্লিক করুন" (Please click here) twice <sup>[67](#o67)</sup>.
   2. Type every detail exactly as on your NID, with no dots in the name and spaces where the NID has them, and press "যাচাই করুন" (Verify) <sup>[67](#o67)</sup>.
   3. Type the OTP (one-time code) sent to your mobile and press Submit, then "সংরক্ষণ করুন ও এগিয়ে যান" (Save and continue), and fill in the full form correctly <sup>[67](#o67)</sup>.
   4. Press "পেমেন্ট করতে এগিয়ে যান" (Proceed to payment), then "পেমেন্ট করুন" (Pay); choose Mobile Banking, then bKash, Nagad or another service, and enter the account number, OTP and PIN <sup>[67](#o67)</sup>.
   5. Download your Registration Card; your BMET registration is then complete <sup>[67](#o67)</sup>.
   6. Take 2 copies of the registration slip to your DEMO (district employment and manpower office) to give your fingerprints <sup>[68](#o68)</sup>.

   **If it fails:** call the OEP hotline, 09614445271 <sup>[69](#o69)</sup>, or Probash-Bondhu, the 24-hour call centre of the Wage Earners' Welfare Board (WEWB, the Ministry's welfare body for migrant workers and their families): 16135 free in Bangladesh, +8809610102030 from abroad <sup>[70](#o70)</sup>.

<a id="step-2-passport-police-clearance-and-certificates"></a>

### 3.3 Step 2: Passport, police clearance and certificates

Get these ready before the employer applies: the Work Permit can't outlast your passport, and the IPA must match it exactly <sup>[52](#o52)</sup>.

1. <a id="step-2-passport-police-clearance-and-certificates-item-1"></a>**Get a passport.**

   You'll need: your original NID; applicants over 20 must use the NID <sup>[71](#o71)</sup>.

   1. Fill in the e-passport application online, exactly as on your NID <sup>[71](#o71)</sup>.
   2. Pay online through A-Challan (the government's online payment-slip system) or the online payment gateways DGePay or ShurjoPay (by card, bKash or Nagad), or at any bank through A-Challan <sup>[72](#o72)</sup>. A 48-page e-passport for 5 years costs Tk 4,025 (regular), Tk 6,325 (express) or Tk 8,625 (super express); for 10 years Tk 5,750, Tk 8,050 or Tk 10,350, VAT included <sup>[72](#o72)</sup>.
   3. Bring the original NID to the divisional passport and visa office or regional passport office for your present address <sup>[71](#o71)</sup>. Delivery after biometrics is within 15 working days (regular), 7 (express) or 2 (super express) <sup>[72](#o72)</sup>.

   **Get a long passport before the employer applies.** A Work Permit is usually issued for 2 years, but it ends 1 month before your passport expires, so a passport with little time left means a shorter permit <sup>[52](#o52)</sup>.

2. <a id="step-2-passport-police-clearance-and-certificates-item-2"></a>**Police clearance certificate (PCC), only if someone asks for it.** MOM's list of documents for issuing a Work Permit has the passport, the immigration document, the signed declaration form, the security bond form and educational certificates where required, and no police clearance <sup>[52](#o52)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Do Bangladeshi agencies or Singapore employers still ask Work Permit or domestic workers for a police clearance, and what for?

   You'll need:
   - a passport valid at least 3 months <sup>[73](#o73)</sup>;
   - to live at your present address, which must be the permanent or emergency address in the passport <sup>[73](#o73)</sup>;
   - a scan of the passport data page attested by a first-class gazetted officer (by the Bangladesh mission if you are abroad) <sup>[73](#o73)</sup>;
   - an attested copy of your NID, birth certificate or ward councillor certificate, if your MRP (machine-readable passport) shows no address <sup>[73](#o73)</sup>.

   1. Open pcc.police.gov.bd, register, log in and press Apply; fill in personal details, then addresses, upload the scans, check and submit; nothing can be changed afterwards <sup>[73](#o73)</sup>.
   2. Pay Tk 1,500 by treasury challan, code 1-7301-0001-2681, at any Bangladesh Bank (the central bank) or Sonali Bank branch, or online by card with a service charge <sup>[73](#o73)</sup>.

      > **Need to know**
      > - Is the fee Tk 1,500 (the portal) or Tk 500 (Bangladesh Police's guidance) <sup>[73](#o73), [74](#o74)</sup>?

   3. The police verify you at your present address; track it by texting "PCC S <reference number>" to 26969 <sup>[73](#o73)</sup>.
   4. Collect it with a copy of your passport or application <sup>[73](#o73)</sup>. If you apply from abroad, someone else can collect it if you upload, with the application, an attested authorisation letter naming them and their NID number <sup>[73](#o73)</sup>. Certificates are issued in English <sup>[74](#o74)</sup>.

   **If it fails:** the PCC helpdesk, Sunday to Thursday 9 am to 5 pm, 01320001824, 01320001825, help.pcc@police.gov.bd <sup>[73](#o73)</sup>; Dhaka Metropolitan Police one-stop service, 01320037339, 01320037329 <sup>[75](#o75)</sup>.

3. <a id="step-2-passport-police-clearance-and-certificates-item-3"></a>**Certificates: an apostille is enough for Singapore.** Singapore is a party to the Hague Apostille Convention (under which member countries accept an apostille certificate instead of embassy legalisation), in force for Singapore since 16 September 2021 <sup>[76](#o76)</sup>. Bangladesh's MOFA (Ministry of Foreign Affairs) lists Singapore among the 114 countries that accepted Bangladesh's accession and recognise Bangladeshi apostilles <sup>[77](#o77)</sup>. MOFA issues an e-Apostille, a certificate with a unique QR code, in five days <sup>[78](#o78)</sup>.

   1. Get the issuing body's verification first: SSC and HSC (Secondary and Higher Secondary School Certificate) by the Education Board, then the Ministry of Education; degrees by the university, then the Ministry of Education <sup>[79](#o79)</sup>. A driving licence goes to a notary and BRTA (the Bangladesh Road Transport Authority), then the Law Ministry <sup>[79](#o79)</sup>; a passport copy, experience, character or NID papers to a notary, then the Law Ministry <sup>[79](#o79)</sup>.
   2. Then MOFA attests or apostilles it <sup>[79](#o79)</sup>. MyGov (mygov.bd, the government's online services portal) lists MOFA's "Certificate attestation for foreign-bound citizens" service, and you check an application's status at mygov.bd (application-latest-status) <sup>[80](#o80), [81](#o81)</sup>.
   3. In Singapore, the Bangladesh High Commission attests Bangladeshi marriage, birth, police, educational and driving-licence certificates only after MOFA in Dhaka has attested them; S$22, by NETS or FlashPay only (NETS is Singapore's debit-card payment network) <sup>[82 (2024)](#o82)</sup>.

   MOM asks for educational certificates only where the pass requires them <sup>[52](#o52)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Does MOM accept a Bangladeshi e-Apostille on educational certificates for S Pass and domestic-worker applications, or does it want them verified some other way?

   **If it fails:** MOFA Bangladesh document authentication, Segunbagicha, Dhaka 1000, +8802-223381852, dgcnw@mofa.gov.bd <sup>[81](#o81)</sup>.

<a id="step-3-the-skills-test"></a>

### 3.4 Step 3: The skills test

<a id="construction-the-seck"></a>

#### 3.4.1 Construction: the SEC(K)

All new construction Work Permit holders must pass the SEC(K) (Skills Evaluation Certificate (Knowledge)), BCA's trade test with a written and a practical part, before working in Singapore <sup>[13](#o13)</sup>. For Bangladeshis, the SEC(K), the SEC (Skills Evaluation Certificate) or the SEC(K) at higher-skill level gives "Basic-Skilled" (R2) status <sup>[63](#o63)</sup>. The test is run by BCA through Overseas Testing Centres (OTCs) that BCA appoints, each listed with a Singapore company behind it <sup>[13](#o13), [10](#o10)</sup>.

**The five BCA-approved OTCs in Bangladesh** (BCA list of 25 September 2025) <sup>[10](#o10)</sup>:
- **Fonda Global SBS Test Centre:** Beroon, Jamgora, Ashulia, Dhaka; Singapore office Fonda Global Engineering, 6481 7255 <sup>[10](#o10)</sup>.
- **Santarli's STL Test Centre:** Kudab, Mirer Bazar, Pubail, Gazipur Sadar; Singapore office Santarli Construction, 6852 3388 <sup>[10](#o10)</sup>.
- **Singapore Engineering & Construction, South Point Skills Centre:** Ghoshbag, Zirabo, Ashulia, Savar, Dhaka; Singapore office Singapore Engineering & Construction, 6546 5289 <sup>[10](#o10)</sup>.
- **ComfortDelgro-Setsco Test Centre:** House 84, Ward 54, Thana Road, Turag, Dhaka-1230; Singapore office Setsco Services <sup>[10](#o10)</sup>.
- **Lian Beng Training and Testing Centre:** 107 Ghoshbag, Zirabo, Ashulia, Dhaka; Singapore office Lian Beng Construction (1988), 6382 2345 <sup>[10](#o10)</sup>.

Each centre tests a fixed list of trades; Fonda tests 24, including plastering, tiling, steel reinforcement, welding and aluminium formwork <sup>[19](#o19)</sup>. There is no test in Bangladesh in July <sup>[83](#o83)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Which BCA OTC is "Progressive Dhaka Technology Ltd", for which the Ministry enlisted a Sending Organisation in December 2025, given that it is not on BCA's September 2025 list <sup>[9](#o9), [10](#o10)</sup>?

**How the test is booked and paid.** Only the Singapore employer (registered in CRS) can register you, through an OTC <sup>[13](#o13)</sup>. The test fee is capped at S$925 before GST and must be paid by the sponsoring employer to the OTC's Singapore office, which gives the employer a receipt <sup>[13](#o13)</sup>. Bangladesh's Ministry rules also say test fees are set by BCA and no extra collection is allowed <sup>[8 (2018)](#o8)</sup>. You can buy BCA's trade syllabus for S$21 a trade <sup>[13](#o13)</sup>.

**What training centres charge.** Training is sold separately: one private centre advertises one-month courses (wiring, rebar, plastering, tiling, welding and others) at Tk 12,000 each and calls itself "government-approved" <sup>[N16](#n16)</sup>. Under the Ministry's rules only approved OTCs and government-run or government-approved technical training centres may train for Singapore, and only the registered test companies may test <sup>[8 (2018)](#o8)</sup>. BMET's training director said its 60 centres train for a nominal fee, but workers go to private centres and costs rise <sup>[N15 (2021)](#n15)</sup>.

**"The centre gives the job."** Some centres sell training, the test and a job as one package <sup>[N15 (2021)](#n15)</sup>. Reported deals: Tk 38,000 admission and Tk 6 lakh in all for a job after 3 months' training (Tongi); Tk 6 lakh after 1.5 months' training, with the passport kept by the centre and Tk 1.5 lakh more demanded at the end (Kapasia) <sup>[N15 (2021)](#n15)</sup>. One worker said his Savar centre made him fail the test because it had no visa for him, and demanded Tk 2 lakh more after he passed; nothing was in writing and no receipts were given <sup>[N15 (2021)](#n15)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - In 2026, what do OTC-linked training centres charge for admission, training and the "package" with a job, and is the package price paid before or after the IPA?
> - How long after passing the test does a worker usually wait for an IPA?

**After you arrive: your certificate.** From 4 May 2026 BCA no longer checks new workers' identity or stamps their IPA, and its Braddell counter is closed <sup>[13](#o13)</sup>. You request your SEC(K) e-certificate yourself on BCA's form (form.gov.sg/682c24da56d1d932efc14a63), logged in with your own Singpass (Singapore's government login, set up after arrival; [Step 8: Work Permit issued, card and SGWorkPass](#step-8-work-permit-issued-card-and-sgworkpass)), never the employer's or agent's, giving your passport number as at the test, the employer and Work Permit number as on the IPA, the trade and test year, and a copy of the IPA; it is emailed within two weeks, and you can make only one request <sup>[13](#o13), [84](#o84)</sup>. Your skill certificates also show under "Training Records" in SGWorkPass, MOM's work pass app ([Step 8: Work Permit issued, card and SGWorkPass](#step-8-work-permit-issued-card-and-sgworkpass)) <sup>[85](#o85)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Can a worker check his own SEC(K) result before travelling anywhere other than with the OTC, which tells candidates their results <sup>[13](#o13)</sup>?

**The test is moving to Singapore.** BCA announced on 30 April 2026 that testing in source countries ends for China and Thailand in January 2027 and for all countries, Bangladesh included, in January 2028 <sup>[86](#o86), [13](#o13)</sup>. From January 2028 <sup>[87](#o87)</sup>:
- the employer must reserve a SEC(K) test slot in Singapore before applying for your Work Permit;
- you get a one-year Work Permit and must pass the SEC(K) in Singapore;
- if you haven't passed by the seventh month, the employer's levy rises by S$600 (to S$1,500 a month for NTS workers);
- if you haven't passed after one year, the permit cannot be renewed.

BCA expects this to cut hiring from four months to about one month <sup>[87](#o87)</sup>. Already, BCA's Alternative Entry Pathway (AEP) lets firms propose their own selection and training process to bring in new construction workers without the overseas test; those workers take the SEC(K) in Singapore <sup>[86](#o86), [88](#o88)</sup>. Firms can also join AEP pilots run by others, such as the Asia Philanthropy Circle's "migrant worker sandbox" <sup>[88](#o88)</sup>. In BMET's March 2025 permission letter for BRAC Probashbandhu (recruiting licence RL1199), a recruiting agency owned by BRAC, the organisation behind this guide, for a "Sandbox Piloting" group, workers take the SEC(K) in Singapore at their own cost <sup>[89](#o89), [90](#o90), [N31](#n31)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Is BRAC Probashbandhu's "Sandbox Piloting" group part of the Asia Philanthropy Circle's AEP sandbox, and how many Bangladeshis have gone through AEP pilots?
> - From January 2028, will Bangladesh's OTCs and their Sending Organisations still control who is sent, and who will pay the Singapore SEC(K) fee?

<a id="higher-skilled-r1-status-in-construction"></a>

#### 3.4.2 Higher-skilled (R1) status in construction

R1 workers cost the employer less levy ([Step 1: Check you qualify and which jobs are open](#step-1-check-you-qualify-and-which-jobs-are-open)) <sup>[7](#o7)</sup>. There are four routes from R2 to R1 <sup>[91](#o91)</sup>:
- **CoreTrade:** at least 4 years in construction and BCA's CoreTrade assessment <sup>[91](#o91)</sup>.
- **Multi-Skilling:** 4 years and a SEC(K) in a second trade, or 6 years and approved safety training <sup>[91](#o91)</sup>.
- **Direct R1:** for Bangladeshis, the SEC(K) at higher-skill level and a fixed monthly salary of at least S$1,600, with no minimum experience; the employer registers you for the higher-level test at an OTC or a Singapore test centre, and the status lasts 2 years, renewable on BCA's CWRS (Construction Workforce Registration System) portal <sup>[91](#o91)</sup>.
- **MBF (Market-Based Skills Recognition Framework):** at least 6 years in construction and a salary of at least S$1,600 <sup>[91](#o91)</sup>.

The new levy starts on the 1st of the next month and shows in WP Online, MOM's employer system <sup>[91](#o91)</sup>.

<a id="marine-shipyard-and-process"></a>

#### 3.4.3 Marine shipyard and process

MOM sets these routes to the lower, higher-skilled levy <sup>[50](#o50), [49](#o49)</sup>:
- **Marine shipyard R1:** the Skills Evaluation Test (SET) Level 1 of Singapore's Institute of Technical Education (ITE) or a National ITE Certificate (Nitec), a trade test endorsed by approved institutions (3G or above in welding), or at least 2 years as a Work Permit holder in Singapore and a salary of at least S$1,200 <sup>[49](#o49)</sup>.
- **Process R1:** 2 WSQ (Workforce Skills Qualifications) courses, 1 Level 2 deep-skilling course and 1 safety course, plus any one of: a fixed monthly salary of at least S$1,200, at least 4 years' employment in Singapore, or work at a firm rated at least Bronze under the Productivity Certification Framework (PCF) <sup>[92](#o92)</sup>. To keep it you must complete a Proficiency Test and Refresher every 2 years for the WSQ and deep-skilling courses, and MOM upgrades you automatically <sup>[92](#o92)</sup>.

<a id="step-4-get-a-real-job-offer"></a>

### 3.5 Step 4: Get a real job offer

<a id="how-the-job-reaches-you"></a>

#### 3.5.1 How the job reaches you

A construction job tested in Bangladesh comes only through the chain described in Background, "The chain, by rule": the OTC's Sending Organisation (SO) sends your bio-data to its one Singapore receiving agent, and BMET clears only IPAs obtained that way <sup>[8 (2018)](#o8)</sup>. The receiving agent in the Ministry's December 2025 enlistment, Hundred Miles Human Resources, holds Singapore EA licence 07C3671, which you can check on MOM's EA Directory (see [Check the Singapore employment agency](#check-the-singapore-employment-agency)) <sup>[9](#o9)</sup>.

In practice, TWC2 often sees IPAs naming a Singapore EA the worker never met; the job came through an intermediary in Bangladesh, another worker or a supervisor at the company <sup>[N32](#n32)</sup>. In MOM's 2024 survey, 56.2% of employers recruited through Singapore EAs and 45.8% through recommendations from other migrant workers <sup>[93](#o93)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Is there a current public list of the Ministry's Singapore Sending Organisations and their OTCs?

<a id="check-the-singapore-employment-agency"></a>

#### 3.5.2 Check the Singapore employment agency

1. Ask for the EA's name; IPAs name the Singapore EA that applied ([Step 5: The employer applies; check your IPA](#step-5-the-employer-applies-check-your-ipa)) <sup>[N32](#n32)</sup>.
2. Search it on MOM's EA Directory, service2.mom.gov.sg/eadirectory (24 hours) <sup>[94](#o94)</sup>. It lists every licensed EA and the name and photo of each registered EA staff member <sup>[95 (2024)](#o95)</sup>.
3. Check the "Under revocation / suspension / surveillance" tab for agencies to avoid <sup>[94](#o94)</sup>.
4. If someone says he works for an EA, ask for his registration card (name, registration number, EA) and match it to the Directory <sup>[95 (2024)](#o95)</sup>. Unlicensed agents charge high fees and may not deliver the job <sup>[95 (2024)](#o95)</sup>.

<a id="check-the-bangladeshi-agency"></a>

#### 3.5.3 Check the Bangladeshi agency

1. <a id="check-the-bangladeshi-agency-item-1"></a>**Is it licensed?** Open www.oep.gov.bd/agencies (free) and find the agency by its RL (recruiting licence) number; the row shows its name, address, office phone, licence status and validity date <sup>[96](#o96)</sup>. A real row reads, for example, "RL2638, S NABIHA OVERSEAS, 46, KAMAL ATATURK AVENUE, (6th FLOOR) Banani C/A, Dhaka-1213 ... Active, 11/Oct/2029" <sup>[96](#o96)</sup>. Go no further unless the status is Active: of 2,863 agencies listed in November 2026, 2,204 were Active and the rest Expired, Cancelled, Suspended or "Server Locked" <sup>[96](#o96)</sup>. A licence can show "Cancelled" while its validity date is years ahead <sup>[96](#o96)</sup>. Recruiting after a licence is cancelled is an offence <sup>[97](#o97)</sup>.
2. <a id="check-the-bangladeshi-agency-item-2"></a>**Is it a Singapore Sending Organisation for your test centre?** BMET clears only IPAs that come through the SO enlisted for that centre (see [How the job reaches you](#how-the-job-reaches-you)) <sup>[8 (2018)](#o8)</sup>. Ask which OTC and which Singapore receiving agent it works with, and check that agent on MOM's EA Directory (see [Check the Singapore employment agency](#check-the-singapore-employment-agency)) <sup>[8 (2018)](#o8), [94](#o94)</sup>.
3. <a id="check-the-bangladeshi-agency-item-3"></a>**What do its papers say?** A BMET permission letter shows the job terms: BRAC Probashbandhu's (RL1199, BRAC's own agency) of March 2025 set a basic salary of S$18 a day for men aged 18 to 36 with HSC, working Monday to Saturday, 44 hours a week <sup>[90](#o90), [89](#o89)</sup>. Which agencies hold Singapore permission letters, and their cited red flags, are in the shared Recruiting agencies list (`agencies/README.md`).
4. <a id="check-the-bangladeshi-agency-item-4"></a>**Who is the broker?** An agency may appoint only a registered person as its sub-agent, and acting as an unregistered sub-agent is an offence <sup>[98 (2023)](#o98)</sup>. Keep the agency's RL number and address and documentary proof of every payment: a complaint to the Ministry needs them <sup>[99](#o99)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Is there a public list of registered sub-agents a worker can check a broker's name against <sup>[98 (2023)](#o98)</sup>?
> - What migration-cost range does BMET's March 2025 permission letter for BRAC Probashbandhu (RL1199) set?

<a id="if-a-relative-or-friend-in-singapore-offers-you-a-job"></a>

#### 3.5.4 If a relative or friend in Singapore offers you a job

Employers often hire on the recommendation of migrant workers <sup>[93](#o93)</sup>, and workers say many Bangladeshis in Singapore sell visas for lakhs of taka <sup>[N15 (2021)](#n15)</sup>. Supervisors who "recommend" new workers to their bosses for a fee are a known source of recruitment fees <sup>[N32](#n32)</sup>. An employer must not take money from you, an agency or anyone else for giving you a job or renewing your permit: this "kickback" is a crime, and the convicted cases are in Background, "Kickbacks and renewal money" <sup>[6](#o6), [28](#o28)</sup>.

<a id="step-5-the-employer-applies-check-your-ipa"></a>

### 3.6 Step 5: The employer applies; check your IPA

1. <a id="step-5-the-employer-applies-check-your-ipa-item-1"></a>**You give written consent** to the employer or EA, which applies online and pays S$35; MOM decides most cases within 1 week <sup>[52](#o52)</sup>. You must not be in Singapore while it applies <sup>[52](#o52)</sup>.
2. <a id="step-5-the-employer-applies-check-your-ipa-item-2"></a>**The employer prints the IPA letter** from myMOM Portal (MOM's online system for employers) and must send you a copy <sup>[52](#o52), [N21](#n21)</sup>. In MOM's 2024 survey, 1 in 10 workers had not received an IPA before starting work <sup>[93](#o93)</sup>.
3. <a id="step-5-the-employer-applies-check-your-ipa-item-3"></a>**Read the salary lines.** The IPA shows your basic salary, fixed allowances, deductions and the resulting fixed monthly salary, your guaranteed pay each month before overtime <sup>[N3](#n3), [N21](#n21)</sup>. Your employer must pay the salary declared to MOM, within 7 days after each month ends, into your bank account if you live in a dormitory <sup>[6](#o6), [100 (2024)](#o100)</sup>. Courts enforce the IPA's terms unless satisfied a different deal was genuinely agreed, so the IPA is often your only proof of salary <sup>[N21](#n21), [N3](#n3)</sup>. In TWC2's 2026 survey, about two in three workers could not correctly state their own IPA salary parts <sup>[N3](#n3)</sup>.
4. <a id="step-5-the-employer-applies-check-your-ipa-item-4"></a>**Check it is real, before paying anything.** Open MOM's "Check work pass and application status", service2.mom.gov.sg/workpass/enquiry: no login, available in Bengali <sup>[101](#o101)</sup>.
   1. Enter your date of birth and passport number, or the FIN (Foreign Identification Number) printed on the IPA <sup>[101](#o101)</sup>.
   2. Read the status of applications made in the last 6 months; to see the salary MOM holds for you, enter the Date of Application from the IPA <sup>[101](#o101)</sup>.
   3. Save the result as a PDF; it is stamped with the date and time of your check <sup>[101](#o101)</sup>.
   4. For a second check, go to BMET in Dhaka or your DEMO: BMET checks job visas free within 3 working days and checks Singapore's online, and has a visa-check app on Google Play <sup>[102](#o102), [61](#o61)</sup>. Bring the visa (for Singapore, the IPA) and, where it applies, a copy of the passport page with the visa stamp <sup>[102](#o102)</sup>.
5. <a id="step-5-the-employer-applies-check-your-ipa-item-5"></a>**If the job or salary differs from what you were promised,** contact the employer or EA before you travel; you may refuse the offer <sup>[101](#o101)</sup>. Your name and passport details on the IPA must match your passport exactly, or you will be refused entry; the employer must correct any error <sup>[52](#o52)</sup>.
6. <a id="step-5-the-employer-applies-check-your-ipa-item-6"></a>**Mind the deadline.** The IPA states how long it is valid: the window to enter Singapore <sup>[103](#o103)</sup>. The employer can extend it once, by up to 1 month, at least 1 week before it expires <sup>[103](#o103)</sup>.
7. <a id="step-5-the-employer-applies-check-your-ipa-item-7"></a>**If MOM rejects the application,** only the employer or the EA that applied can appeal, in myMOM Portal, within 30 days of the rejection; MOM won't take an appeal from you, and 90% of appeals are decided within 3 weeks <sup>[104](#o104)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How long is a Work Permit IPA usually valid from its date of issue?

<a id="step-6-before-you-fly"></a>

### 3.7 Step 6: Before you fly

**What the employer does** (all before your flight) <sup>[52](#o52), [30](#o30), [48](#o48), [55](#o55)</sup>:
- submits an online form for MOM's pre-entry housing check (for male CMP workers);
- after MOM approves the housing, books your Onboard centre slot at least 4 days before arrival, and only then your flight <sup>[54](#o54)</sup>;
- buys the S$5,000 security bond, which must be in effect on the day you land; if it is not, immigration refuses entry and you are sent home <sup>[30](#o30)</sup>;
- buys your medical insurance and Primary Care Plan.

Ask for a copy of the security bond acknowledgement letter, which MOM tells employers to send you <sup>[30](#o30)</sup>; in a 2026 recruitment scam a worker was sent a bond document that the employer said was forged ([Scams](#scams)) <sup>[N33](#n33)</sup>.

**What you do in Bangladesh.** MOM's medical examination is done in Singapore after arrival ([Step 7: Arrival and the Onboard centre](#step-7-arrival-and-the-onboard-centre)) <sup>[105 (2024)](#o105)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Do Singapore employers or agencies require a medical test in Bangladesh before the IPA, at which centres, and at what cost?

1. <a id="step-6-before-you-fly-item-1"></a>**Do the compulsory 3-day pre-departure orientation (PDO)** at one of 70 training institutes: apply on OEP and pay the institute's fee against a money receipt <sup>[102](#o102), [61](#o61)</sup>. A Dhaka agency's Singapore checklist says only first-time workers take it, and booking it needs your BMET registration and passport number <sup>[N34](#n34)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - What is the click path for booking the PDO on OEP?

2. <a id="step-6-before-you-fly-item-2"></a>**No IPA attestation is needed any more.** Since 18 April 2025 the Bangladesh High Commission in Singapore no longer attests IPAs, by a Ministry of Expatriates' Welfare decision of 17 April 2025 (memo 49.00.0000.048.11.017.21.119) <sup>[106](#o106)</sup>.

3. <a id="step-6-before-you-fly-item-3"></a>**Get BMET clearance (the smart card).** The smart card is your emigration clearance.
