With it you need no separate Welfare Board membership, your family gets a Tk 3 lakh grant from the Board if you die in the country the clearance is for, and you are insured ([Step 6, item 4: Know your insurance](#step-6-before-you-fly-item-4)) <sup>[107](#o107), [108](#o108), [61](#o61)</sup>.
   In practice, for Singapore an agency in Dhaka lists: the IPA, the passport, an updated BMET registration, fingerprints given in your own district, and the PDO certificate if it is your first time, or, if you have gone before, the passport's photo page and the page with your last clearance and agency (RL) stamp <sup>[N34](#n34)</sup>. Without that old stamp page, a returning worker has to do the training again <sup>[N34](#n34)</sup>.
   1. **If an agency recruited you,** it applies after the visa with its application, the visa copy, the attested contract (where applicable), an attested copy of your registration card, its undertaking on job certainty on Tk 300 non-judicial stamp paper, the original bank pay order for the welfare and smart card fees, and the original source income-tax challan; BMET's emigration branch clears within 3 working days <sup>[102](#o102)</sup>.
   2. **If you got the visa yourself,** apply at BMET's emigration branch in Dhaka or the Chattogram district employment and manpower office with your application, the visa copy, the contract (if any), an attested copy of your registration card, a declaration on Tk 300 stamp paper and pay orders for the welfare and insurance fees; 3 working days <sup>[102](#o102)</sup>. A construction IPA that came through a test centre is cleared only through the SO enlisted for that centre ([Step 4: Get a real job offer](#step-4-get-a-real-job-offer)) <sup>[8 (2018)](#o8)</sup>.
   3. **Fees:**
      - welfare fee: Tk 5,500 from 1 October 2026 <sup>[109](#o109)</sup>;
      - insurance premium: Tk 1,000, once <sup>[61](#o61)</sup>;
      - clearance fee: Tk 250 per worker <sup>[102](#o102)</sup>;
      - source income tax: Tk 400 (single visa, not attested) or Tk 250 (attested), and Tk 1,200 (skilled) or Tk 800 (unskilled) on a group visa <sup>[102](#o102)</sup>;
      - smart card fee: Tk 250 <sup>[102](#o102)</sup>; on the agency route it is paid by pay order with the welfare fee <sup>[102](#o102)</sup>.

      > **Need to know**
      > - Did the insurance premium change with the welfare fee rise <sup>[110](#o110)</sup>?

   **Check it is real** at portal.wewb.gov.bd/bmet-wewbm-check <sup>[107](#o107)</sup>. Welfare desks at Dhaka, Chattogram and Sylhet airports check smart cards before departure <sup>[102](#o102)</sup>; in 2023-24 they checked 1,391,988 and stopped 1,098 travellers with no or fake clearance <sup>[111 (2024)](#o111)</sup>. **If it goes wrong:** BMET's Director (Emigration), +8802222222415, 01711-135901, diremi@bmet.gov.bd; then the Additional Director General (Employment), 02-48321949, adgemp@bmet.gov.bd <sup>[102](#o102)</sup>; complaints against an agency go by application with proof to BMET's Director General, free, 30 working days <sup>[102](#o102)</sup>.

4. <a id="step-6-before-you-fly-item-4"></a>**Know your insurance.** Workers who leave with BMET clearance on or after 12 March 2023 are insured by Jiban Bima Corporation (JBC), under its contract with the Welfare Board, for Tk 10 lakh, for 5 years from the smart card's date, for workers aged 18 to 55, for the one-time Tk 1,000 <sup>[61](#o61), [112 (2024)](#o112)</sup>. It pays on death, permanent disability, and job loss with return within 6 months of going (job loss is not covered for a worker who went entirely at the employer's cost); what it pays and how to claim are under If a worker dies, is injured or loses the job <sup>[112 (2024)](#o112), [61](#o61)</sup>. You can download your insurance certificate from JBC's e-service <sup>[112 (2024)](#o112)</sup>.

5. <a id="step-6-before-you-fly-item-5"></a>**If you need a loan,** PKB (Probashi Kallyan Bank, the government bank for migrant workers) lends up to Tk 3 lakh at 9% simple interest a year, repaid in equal monthly instalments over up to 3 years including a grace period, and decides within 7 working days of complete papers <sup>[113](#o113), [114 (2019)](#o114)</sup>. Women migrants have a separate loan on the same terms <sup>[115](#o115)</sup>. You must be 18 to 60 and not in default on any loan, and you and your guarantors must live permanently in the branch's area <sup>[113](#o113)</sup>. A worker going back on a re-entry visa can also borrow, adding proof of remitting through legal channels, proof of employment abroad and a statement of migration costs <sup>[113](#o113)</sup>.

   You'll need <sup>[113](#o113)</sup>:
   - the free application form;
   - 3 recent photos;
   - copies of your NID, passport, visa and BMET card, and the contract if you have one;
   - 3 signed MICR (magnetic-ink, machine-readable) cheque leaves from your own active bank account, with its statement;
   - a PKB savings account opened with at least Tk 500;
   - two guarantors, each with a photo, an NID copy and proof of permanent address <sup>[113](#o113)</sup>:
     - one financially solvent person, with 3 signed MICR cheques, a statement and proof of income <sup>[113](#o113)</sup>;
     - one family member <sup>[113](#o113)</sup>.

   1. Apply at the PKB branch for your permanent address, or online on mygov.bd, uploading your photo, passport, visa, BMET card and NID and your guarantors' NIDs and photos; you can apply only once until the loan is approved or refused, and you pay nothing when you submit <sup>[113](#o113), [114 (2019)](#o114)</sup>.
   2. After the decision, pay processing and documentation fees of 0.5% each plus VAT, and Tk 900 per lakh for risk cover, plus stamps <sup>[113](#o113), [114 (2019)](#o114)</sup>.

   Agrani Bank also lends Tk 50,000 to Tk 3 lakh to people aged 18-45 who have a job visa or are preparing to go, at 11.90% interest over 15-18 months, guaranteed by the worker and a parent or sibling <sup>[N35](#n35)</sup>.

   **If it goes wrong:** PKB helpline 16238, +880 9677-787878 from abroad <sup>[113](#o113)</sup>; loans department 01716-175560, migration@pkb.gov.bd <sup>[116](#o116)</sup>; the grievance officer, GM (Admin and Accounts), +88-02-48321195, gm2@pkb.gov.bd (30 working days), then dmd@pkb.gov.bd, then md@pkb.gov.bd <sup>[116](#o116)</sup>.

6. <a id="step-6-before-you-fly-item-6"></a>**The flight.** Before you go, check you have <sup>[61](#o61), [N34](#n34)</sup>:
   - a valid passport;
   - the IPA letter;
   - the medical certificate (where it applies);
   - the agency's name, address and phone;
   - the BMET clearance or smart card;
   - the air ticket.

   Submit the SG Arrival Card (SGAC) online, on ICA's website or the MyICA app, within 3 days before you land, counting the arrival day <sup>[117](#o117)</sup>. It is free, and ICA does not endorse anyone who charges to submit it for you <sup>[117](#o117)</sup>.

   Keep one set of copies of all papers at home and at least 3 with you, and open at least 2 bank accounts before going, one in your name and one in a family member's <sup>[61](#o61)</sup>. Never carry a packet for anyone whose contents you don't know <sup>[61](#o61)</sup>.

**Carry** (to the Onboard centre) <sup>[54](#o54)</sup>:
- the full IPA letter, printed or on your phone;
- your passport;
- any vaccination records;
- a working smartphone (you get a free Singapore SIM at the centre).

<a id="step-7-arrival-and-the-onboard-centre"></a>

### 3.8 Step 7: Arrival and the Onboard centre

On arrival show your IPA letter to the immigration officer <sup>[52](#o52)</sup>. If you are a male CMP worker, the employer must take you straight from the airport to the Onboard centre, Onboard@Sengkang West, 20A Seletar West Road 1, Singapore 798991 <sup>[54](#o54)</sup>. You stay there 3 days, or 2 if you are returning within 2 years of your last pass ending; the employer pays for your food and bed <sup>[54](#o54)</sup>.

1. <a id="step-7-arrival-and-the-onboard-centre-item-1"></a>**Day 1, check-in:** your papers and booking are checked and you declare your health <sup>[54](#o54)</sup>.
2. <a id="step-7-arrival-and-the-onboard-centre-item-2"></a>**Day 2, medical:** an enhanced medical examination for infectious diseases and fitness for work and dormitory life, and a check of your vaccination records <sup>[54](#o54)</sup>. It screens for tuberculosis, HIV, syphilis and malaria <sup>[105 (2024)](#o105)</sup>. If you are found medically unfit, the Work Permit is revoked <sup>[6](#o6)</sup>. Abnormal findings other than colour vision need a follow-up examination <sup>[54](#o54)</sup>. The medical provider sends the results to MOM and the employer is emailed, usually within a week of checkout; copies of the report come from Healthway Medical Group, WhatsApp 9750 9541 or mwocsk@healthwaymedical.com, 9 am to 4 pm daily <sup>[54](#o54)</sup>.
3. <a id="step-7-arrival-and-the-onboard-centre-item-3"></a>**Day 3, Settling-in Programme (SIP):** a compulsory 1-day course, in Bengali, on Singapore's laws, your employment rights, work safety and injury compensation, key apps, sending money home and avoiding illegal moneylenders, and where to get medical care <sup>[54](#o54), [118](#o118)</sup>. Women CMP workers and manufacturing workers attend it instead within 2 weeks at the MWC (Migrant Workers' Centre) Recreation Club @ Soon Lee <sup>[118](#o118)</sup>.

Construction workers must then take the Construction Safety Orientation Course (CSOC) within 2 weeks and pass it within 3 months, or the permit may be revoked; shipyard workers take the SSICGT (Shipyard Safety Instruction Course for Workers, General Trade), and process workers the OPSOC (Oil Petroleum Safety Orientation Course) <sup>[7](#o7), [49](#o49), [50](#o50)</sup>.

<a id="step-8-work-permit-issued-card-and-sgworkpass"></a>

### 3.9 Step 8: Work Permit issued, card and SGWorkPass

1. <a id="step-8-work-permit-issued-card-and-sgworkpass-item-1"></a>**The employer issues the permit within 14 days of your arrival** and pays S$35 <sup>[52](#o52)</sup>. You must finish all the post-arrival steps in your IPA within those 14 days <sup>[54](#o54)</sup>. The employer gets a notification letter, valid 1 month, which lets you start work and travel while you wait for the card, and says whether you must register fingerprints <sup>[52](#o52)</sup>.
2. <a id="step-8-work-permit-issued-card-and-sgworkpass-item-2"></a>**If required, register fingerprints and photo within 1 week** at MOM Services Centre, Hall C, by appointment the employer makes <sup>[52](#o52)</sup>. Bring your original passport, the appointment letter, the notification letter and a phone with a Singapore number and the Singpass and SGWorkPass apps installed <sup>[52](#o52)</sup>. MOM takes your fingerprints and photo, opens your Singpass account (Singapore's government login) and helps you set up both apps <sup>[52](#o52)</sup>.
3. <a id="step-8-work-permit-issued-card-and-sgworkpass-item-3"></a>**Set up SGWorkPass,** MOM's app for Android, iPhone and Huawei, with a step-by-step guide in Bengali <sup>[85](#o85)</sup>. Log in with Singpass to see your digital work pass and employment details; tap "Employment Info" to see the salary MOM holds for you, updated 1 working day after the employer reports a change <sup>[52](#o52), [85](#o85)</sup>. Show the digital pass or the card whenever a public officer asks <sup>[6](#o6)</sup>.
4. <a id="step-8-work-permit-issued-card-and-sgworkpass-item-4"></a>**Receive the card** within 5 working days of registration, at the address the employer gave <sup>[52](#o52)</sup>. After 2 failed deliveries it can be collected at MOM Services Centre, Hall C, after 3 working days <sup>[52](#o52)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Does MOM still tell employers, on its "Apply for a Work Permit" page, to book a BCA identity check within 4 months of the SEC(K) test, although BCA closed that counter on 4 May 2026 <sup>[52](#o52), [13](#o13)</sup>?

<a id="where-to-get-help-by-stage"></a>

### 3.10 Where to get help, by stage

- **In Bangladesh, before you pay or fly:** complain about a recruiting agency to BMET's Director General ([Step 6, item 3: Get BMET clearance (the smart card)](#step-6-before-you-fly-item-3)) <sup>[102](#o102)</sup>, or to the Ministry's enforcement branch with an application, documentary proof, your full address and the agency's RL number and address; it is free and settled within 30 working days (sasenforcement@probashi.gov.bd, 02-41030255) <sup>[99](#o99)</sup>. The Ministry's Training Section-1, which enlists Singapore SOs: +88-02-41030251, astraining@probashi.gov.bd <sup>[9](#o9)</sup>. If a BMET officer does not resolve it, BMET's grievance route runs to its complaint officer, the Director (Employment), diremi@bmet.gov.bd, 01711111544 (3 months), then the Ministry's Secretary, secretary@probashi.gov.bd (1 month), then the Cabinet Division's grievance system, www.grs.gov.bd <sup>[102](#o102)</sup>.
- **A crime in Bangladesh:** under the Overseas Employment and Migrants Act 2013, taking money on a false promise of a job abroad, or keeping a migrant's passport or papers, carries up to 5 years' jail and a fine of at least Tk 1 lakh; these offences are cognisable and non-bailable <sup>[97](#o97)</sup>.
- **About a Singapore EA or an IPA:** report overcharging or other EA breaches on MOM's online form, form.gov.sg/64e5cb37f8c88e0012480ed1; information is kept confidential <sup>[29 (2024)](#o29), [94](#o94)</sup>. Workers asked for kickbacks can call MOM on 6438 5122 or the Migrant Workers' Centre on 6536 2692 <sup>[28](#o28)</sup>. Anyone can report a breach through MOM's "Report an infringement" eService <sup>[28](#o28)</sup>. MOM never asks you to transfer money or give bank log-ins; if unsure whether something is a scam, call the 24/7 ScamShield Helpline on 1799 <sup>[94](#o94)</sup>.
- **In Singapore, through Bangladesh:** the Bangladesh High Commission takes complaints and works them through MOM, the Singapore Police Force, the employer and migrant-worker groups; its address is under Contacts <sup>[5](#o5)</sup>.
- **What actually happens:** when the agents were overseas and no Singapore EA was involved, MOM told one cheated worker in 2026 that little could be done, and the police treated it as a civil matter; she left Singapore without her money <sup>[N33](#n33)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Where does a cheated worker file a criminal case under the 2013 Act (the local police station, a court or BMET), and has any Singapore broker been convicted under it?

<a id="scams"></a>

### 3.11 Scams

<a id="done-to-workers"></a>

#### 3.11.1 Done to workers

**Fake agent who "applies for your IPA":** A person in Singapore says he works for a licensed EA and asks your relative there to send your details and pay into his bank account for an IPA; in 2026 a Bangladeshi construction worker was jailed 13 months and 3 weeks for taking over S$35,000 from 17 workers this way <sup>[N36](#n36)</sup>. Victims came through a friend's introduction <sup>[N36](#n36)</sup>. **Spot it:** only an employer or its EA can apply, and a construction worker cannot <sup>[48](#o48), [N36](#n36)</sup>; check the EA and its staff card on the EA Directory ([Step 4: Get a real job offer](#step-4-get-a-real-job-offer)) <sup>[95 (2024)](#o95)</sup>.

**"Approved" screenshot, then a different IPA:** In 2026 an overseas agent sent a worker promised a hotel job in Singapore a screenshot showing her pass "approved", took her money, and only then sent an IPA with a different job and a lower salary, saying the promised job was still hers <sup>[N33](#n33)</sup>. **Spot it:** check the application yourself on MOM's portal before paying, and refuse an IPA that differs from the offer ([Step 5: The employer applies; check your IPA](#step-5-the-employer-applies-check-your-ipa)) <sup>[101](#o101)</sup>.

**Lower salary on the IPA than agreed:** TWC2 has seen employers agree a salary with the worker but declare less to MOM, so the IPA shows the lower figure <sup>[N21](#n21)</sup>. In MOM's 2024 survey 2.5% of workers were paid less than their IPA salary <sup>[N21](#n21)</sup>. **Spot it:** read the salary lines and compare them with MOM's record ([Step 5: The employer applies; check your IPA](#step-5-the-employer-applies-check-your-ipa), items 3 and 4) <sup>[101](#o101)</sup>.

**Fake security bond or a cancelled IPA:** In the same 2026 case the agent then sent a PDF "security bond"; no one met the worker at the airport, the IPA was cancelled, and the employer said it had not paid the bond and the document was forged <sup>[N33](#n33)</sup>. Once an IPA is cancelled you cannot work <sup>[103](#o103)</sup>. **Spot it:** the employer buys the bond from a bank or insurer <sup>[30](#o30)</sup>; ask the employer for MOM's acknowledgement letter ([Step 6: Before you fly](#step-6-before-you-fly)) <sup>[30](#o30)</sup>.

**Fake work permits and "training fees":** In 2026 a man in Singapore allegedly took over S$14,000 from six Indian workers for non-existent work permits, gave them forged MOM documents, said "MOM rules have changed", charged five of them S$854 each for a "training course" and asked for bank deposits <sup>[N37](#n37)</sup>. **Spot it:** training and medical checks in Singapore are the employer's cost ([Who pays what](#who-pays-what)) <sup>[58 (2011)](#o58)</sup>; check the FIN on any IPA you are sent on MOM's portal ([Step 5: The employer applies; check your IPA](#step-5-the-employer-applies-check-your-ipa)), as the victims' contact did, finding that the FINs did not exist <sup>[N37](#n37)</sup>.

**Training centre that holds your passport and moves the price:** Workers have reported centres that promise a Singapore job for a fixed price, keep the passport, demand lakhs more after training, or fail a worker on purpose until they have a visa for him; three workers said at least Tk 7 to 8 lakh was extracted from them beyond the deal and they still did not get the promised job <sup>[N15 (2021)](#n15)</sup>. **Spot it:** test fees are the employer's to pay and capped ([Step 3: The skills test](#step-3-the-skills-test)) <sup>[13](#o13), [8 (2018)](#o8)</sup>; the worker given no receipts had no proof of what he paid <sup>[N15 (2021)](#n15)</sup>.

**"Train with us, earn Tk 1 lakh a month" adverts:** Facebook pages advertise Singapore training with jobs at Tk 1 lakh a month, and most of their phone numbers are switched off <sup>[N15 (2021)](#n15)</sup>. Publishing a job-abroad advertisement without the government's or BMET's prior approval is itself an offence in Bangladesh: up to 1 year's jail and a fine of at least Tk 50,000 <sup>[97](#o97)</sup>. **Spot it:** only BCA-appointed OTCs give the test ([Step 3: The skills test](#step-3-the-skills-test)) <sup>[13](#o13)</sup>; in TWC2's 2026 survey, Bangladeshis in their first 2 years averaged S$526 a month (about Tk 51,000) <sup>[N3](#n3), [1](#o1)</sup>.

**Kickbacks to keep or renew your job:** Your boss or supervisor asks for money to renew your permit <sup>[28](#o28)</sup>. It is an offence (see [If a relative or friend in Singapore offers you a job](#if-a-relative-or-friend-in-singapore-offers-you-a-job)) <sup>[28](#o28)</sup>; call MOM on 6438 5122 <sup>[28](#o28)</sup>.

**Phantom-worker companies:** In March 2026 MOM arrested 10 people, among them directors and employees of five construction companies that allegedly paid CPF (Central Provident Fund, Singapore's pension scheme) contributions to "phantom" local workers to raise their quota for foreign workers <sup>[119](#o119)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What happened to the workers of the five construction companies in MOM's March 2026 phantom-worker case?

<a id="tried-by-workers"></a>

#### 3.11.2 Tried by workers

False declarations in a work pass application carry a fine of up to S$20,000, up to 2 years in jail, or both, and the applicant can be barred from working in Singapore <sup>[119](#o119)</sup>. Buying or selling a visa or work permit inside Bangladesh, or getting one by illegal means, is a crime under Bangladesh's 2013 Act: 2 to 7 years' rigorous imprisonment and a fine of at least Tk 3 lakh <sup>[97](#o97)</sup>. The SEC(K) e-certificate must be requested with the worker's own Singpass and the passport number used at the test <sup>[84](#o84)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Have workers been caught sending someone else to sit the SEC(K) at a Bangladesh OTC, and what happens to them?

<a id="your-rights-at-work"></a>

### 3.12 Your rights at work

<a id="contract-and-which-law-covers-you"></a>

#### 3.12.1 Contract and which law covers you

**Which laws apply:** Two laws protect a Work Permit holder <sup>[120](#o120)</sup>. The Employment Act, Singapore's main labour law, covers local and foreign employees who work under a contract of service <sup>[120](#o120)</sup>. The Employment of Foreign Manpower Act (EFMA) also covers every work pass holder and sets out the employer's responsibilities and obligations in employing foreigners <sup>[120](#o120)</sup>. Part 4 of the Employment Act, which sets hours, rest days and overtime, covers a "workman" (someone whose work is mainly manual labour, such as a construction worker, cleaner, labourer, machine operator or lorry driver) earning a basic salary of S$4,500 a month or less <sup>[120](#o120)</sup>.

**Your papers:**

- **The IPA letter.** The In-Principle Approval (IPA) letter that MOM (the Ministry of Manpower) issues before you travel (see [Step 5: The employer applies; check your IPA](#step-5-the-employer-applies-check-your-ipa)) states your employer, job, basic and fixed monthly salary, fixed allowances, monthly deductions and housing <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>; the employer must keep to it and cannot change it without your permission <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>. Keep a copy for the whole job, and go to MOM if your salary is cut without your written consent <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>.
- **Key Employment Terms (KETs).** The employer must give you your main terms in writing: salary, pay date, overtime, working hours, rest days, leave and medical benefits <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>.
- **The Work Permit itself.** You may work only for the employer, in the job and in the sector printed on your Work Permit (see [What can get you jailed or deported](#what-can-get-you-jailed-or-deported)) <sup>[6](#o6)</sup>.
- **Check what MOM holds.** The free SGWorkPass app (see [The card and the SGWorkPass app](#the-card-and-the-sgworkpass-app)) shows, under "Employment Info", the monthly salary your employer declared to MOM, updated 1 working day after the employer notifies a change <sup>[85](#o85)</sup>.

<a id="pay-and-deductions"></a>

#### 3.12.2 Pay and deductions

**No minimum wage: the IPA salary is your wage:** Singapore has no minimum wage <sup>[122](#o122)</sup>. What protects you is the salary in your IPA: the employer must pay a Work Permit holder the fixed monthly salary it declared to MOM <sup>[100 (2024)](#o100)</sup>, and must pay at least that amount even when there is no work, except while you are on no-pay leave outside Singapore <sup>[123 (2020)](#o123)</sup>. It cannot lower your basic salary or fixed allowances, or add or raise deductions, without your written consent and without first telling MOM <sup>[124 (2024)](#o124)</sup>. Employers pay no CPF (Central Provident Fund, Singapore's pension scheme) for Work Permit holders, so nothing should be deducted for it <sup>[100 (2024)](#o100)</sup>.

**When and how you are paid:**

- **Salary:** at least once a month, no later than 7 days after the end of the month <sup>[100 (2024)](#o100)</sup>.
- **Overtime pay:** within 14 days after the end of the salary period <sup>[122](#o122)</sup>.
- **Into a bank account:** if you live in a dormitory, salary must go straight into your bank account; outside dormitories, cash is allowed <sup>[100 (2024)](#o100)</sup>, but the employer must pay into your bank account if you ask it to <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>. If you agree that the employer keeps your bank book, it must let you check it <sup>[100 (2024)](#o100)</sup>.
- **Payslip:** an itemised payslip with each payment, or within three working days of it, showing basic salary, allowances, deductions, overtime hours and overtime pay <sup>[125 (2024)](#o125), [121](#o121)</sup>.

Never sign blank papers, payment vouchers or payslips you don't understand or disagree with; if the employer asks you to, go to MOM <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>.

**What may and may not be deducted:** The employer may never deduct, under any circumstances <sup>[124 (2024)](#o124)</sup>:
- money as a condition of employing you or keeping you on;
- the cost of renewing your work pass;
- the security bond (a S$5,000 guarantee the employer must buy for each non-Malaysian worker) <sup>[30](#o30)</sup>;
- your medical insurance;
- your ticket home (repatriation);
- compulsory training;
- medical fees;
- the foreign worker levy (the monthly charge the employer pays MOM for each foreign worker) <sup>[6](#o6)</sup>.

Deductions that are allowed have limits:
- **All deductions together:** no more than 50% of the salary for the period, not counting deductions for absence or for repaying an advance or loan <sup>[124 (2024)](#o124)</sup>.
- **Damage to or loss of the employer's goods or money:** only after an inquiry where you can explain, at most 25% of one month's salary, as a one-time deduction <sup>[124 (2024)](#o124)</sup>.
- **Housing or amenities you accepted:** no more than their value and no more than 25% of the salary for the period <sup>[124 (2024)](#o124)</sup>.

The employer may not demand or take money from you as a condition of employing you (a "kickback") <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>; anyone found to have collected kickbacks can be fined up to S$30,000, jailed for up to 2 years, or both, and workers asked for one can call MOM on 6438 5122 or the Migrant Workers' Centre on 6536 2692 <sup>[28](#o28)</sup>.

If your salary is cut without your consent, call MOM on 6438 5122 or go to the MOM Services Centre, 1500 Bendemeer Road, Singapore 339946 <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>; if it is late or unpaid, follow If you aren't paid under If something goes wrong <sup>[100 (2024)](#o100), [121](#o121)</sup>.

<a id="hours-rest-and-leave"></a>

#### 3.12.3 Hours, rest and leave

**Hours and overtime:**

- **Normal hours:** up to 8 hours a day or 44 a week if you work more than 5 days a week; up to 9 hours a day or 44 a week if you work 5 days or fewer <sup>[126](#o126)</sup>.
- **Breaks:** you are generally not required to work more than 6 hours in a row without a break <sup>[126](#o126)</sup>.
- **Overtime:** at least 1.5 times your hourly basic rate <sup>[126](#o126)</sup>. The hourly basic rate is (12 x monthly basic pay) divided by (52 x 44) <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>.
- **Limits:** no more than 12 hours of work a day, except in emergencies such as an accident or urgent work on machinery <sup>[126](#o126)</sup>, or up to 14 hours if the employer has an overtime exemption from MOM <sup>[126](#o126)</sup>; and no more than 72 overtime hours a month unless the employer has an exemption <sup>[126](#o126)</sup>.
- **Records:** the employer must record your overtime; check the record and sign only if it is right <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>. Keep your payslips and time sheets <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>.

**Rest day and public holidays:**

- **Rest day:** 1 a week, unpaid <sup>[126](#o126)</sup>; no more than 12 days may pass between two rest days <sup>[126](#o126)</sup>. The employer cannot make you work on it except in exceptional circumstances <sup>[126](#o126)</sup>.
- **Rest-day work at the employer's request:** 1 day's salary for up to half a normal day, 2 days' salary for more than half, and 2 days' salary plus overtime pay beyond the normal day <sup>[126](#o126)</sup>.
- **Public holidays:** 11 paid holidays a year <sup>[127](#o127)</sup>; work on one earns an extra day's salary at the basic rate <sup>[127](#o127)</sup>. If a holiday falls on your rest day, the next working day is a paid holiday <sup>[127](#o127)</sup>. Being absent without reason on the working day before or after a holiday loses the holiday pay <sup>[127](#o127)</sup>.

**Annual leave:** Paid annual leave starts after 3 months with the employer <sup>[128 (2023)](#o128)</sup>: 7 days in the first year, rising by one day each year to 14 days from the eighth year <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>.

**Sick leave and hospitalisation leave:** After 3 months with the employer you get paid outpatient sick leave and paid hospitalisation leave <sup>[129](#o129)</sup>. The yearly entitlement grows with service: 5 days outpatient and 15 days hospitalisation after 3 months, 8 and 30 after 4 months, 11 and 45 after 5 months, and 14 and 60 from 6 months, the 60 days including the 14 <sup>[121](#o121), [129](#o129)</sup>. To be paid you need a medical certificate (MC) from a registered doctor or dentist, and you must tell the employer within 48 hours of being absent <sup>[129](#o129)</sup>. Sick leave after a work accident is paid under different rules, set out under If you are injured at work <sup>[130](#o130)</sup>.

<a id="medical-care"></a>

#### 3.12.4 Medical care

**Who pays:** The employer must let you see a doctor and pays your medical bills <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>. Get an MC and a receipt for every visit, give them to the employer and keep a photo of each <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>. If the employer refuses to arrange or pay for treatment, report it to MOM on 6438 5122 <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>.

**The medical insurance your employer must buy:** The employer must buy medical insurance covering inpatient care and day surgery of at least S$60,000 a year for each Work Permit holder, including for illness that has nothing to do with work <sup>[55](#o55)</sup>. You pay nothing for it <sup>[55](#o55)</sup>. For bills above S$15,000 the insurer pays 75% and the employer 25% <sup>[55](#o55)</sup>, and for policies starting from 1 July 2025 the insurer pays the hospital directly once the claim is accepted <sup>[55](#o55)</sup>.

The only cost you can be asked to share is a co-payment of hospital bills, and only if all of these hold <sup>[55](#o55)</sup>:
- it is no more than 10% of your fixed monthly salary a month;
- it lasts no more than 6 months in every 2 years of employment;
- it is written in your employment contract or collective agreement and you agreed to it.

**The Primary Care Plan (PCP):** The PCP is a yearly plan the employer must buy for you if you live in a dormitory for 7 or more workers or work in the Construction, Marine shipyard or Process (CMP) sectors, before your Work Permit is issued or renewed <sup>[131](#o131)</sup>. It covers <sup>[56](#o56)</sup>:
- unlimited consultations for illnesses such as fever, flu, high blood pressure and diabetes;
- one basic health screening a year;
- telemedicine (a video call with a doctor) and medicine delivery;
- scheduled transport between your dormitory and the medical centre when you report sick.

You pay S$5 per clinic visit and S$2 per telemedicine consultation <sup>[57](#o57)</sup>. PCP centres and clinics are closed on public holidays <sup>[56](#o56)</sup>. From 1 April 2027, four Anchor Operators (the medical providers MOM appoints) will run four zones (SATA CommHealth in the North, St Andrew's Mission Hospital in the South, Healthway Medical Group in the East, Fullerton Health in the West), with a clinic within 2km of most workers, advance booking, and S$5 for both clinic and telemedicine consultations <sup>[57](#o57)</sup>.

<a id="housing"></a>

#### 3.12.5 Housing

**Your right to decent housing:** The employer must give you acceptable housing <sup>[123 (2020)](#o123)</sup>. You must live at the address the employer registered with MOM and tell the employer if you move, or your work pass can be revoked <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>. Report overcrowding, poor sanitation or ventilation, pests or unrepaired facilities to MOM on 6438 5122 <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>; in a dormitory you can also report defects to the operator in the free DormWatch app, which keeps MOM informed <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>.

**Dormitories:** Dormitories are licensed under the Foreign Employee Dormitories Act (FEDA); about 1,600 are licensed, and only 30% meet the New Dormitory Standards (NDS) with 12 or fewer people per room <sup>[132](#o132)</sup>. The other 70% are older dormitories that typically put 12 to 16 people in a room <sup>[132](#o132)</sup>. FEDA-licensed dormitories must give each resident a bed and a lockable cabinet, and Wi-Fi in common and living areas <sup>[133 (2022)](#o133)</sup>.

The standards are being raised in stages:
- **Older dormitories (today):** no limit on people per room, at least 3.5 square metres per person on average, and one toilet, shower, sink and urinal per 15 residents <sup>[134](#o134)</sup>.
- **By 2030, the Dormitory Transition Scheme (DTS):** no more than 12 per room, at least 3.6 square metres per person and one toilet, shower and basin per 6 residents <sup>[134](#o134)</sup>. Dormitories whose government leases end in 2033 or earlier are exempt <sup>[134](#o134)</sup>.
- **By 2040, the NDS:** no more than 12 per room, at least 1 metre between beds and at least 4.2 square metres per person <sup>[134](#o134)</sup>.
- **A relaxation from 1 January 2027 to 31 January 2030:** NDS dormitories may go down to 3.6 square metres per person and up to 14 per room, but the 1 metre between beds stays <sup>[135](#o135)</sup>.

**Other housing:** You may also be housed in a private home, such as a condominium, landed house or shophouse unit <sup>[136](#o136)</sup>. An HDB (Housing and Development Board, public housing) flat is open to a non-Malaysian Work Permit holder only in the services sector, or as a rented bedroom (not a whole flat) in manufacturing, so not to construction, marine shipyard or process workers <sup>[136](#o136)</sup>. Private homes are capped at 6 unrelated occupants, and whole HDB flats of 3 rooms or more at 6 occupants; from 22 January 2024 to 31 December 2028 the cap is 8 for 4-room or bigger HDB flats and for private homes of 90 square metres or more with URA (Urban Redevelopment Authority) approval <sup>[136](#o136)</sup>. In an HDB flat, you can check in HDB's eService that the owner has registered you as a tenant <sup>[136](#o136)</sup>. If there are cameras, you must be told where they are, and they must not be in bathrooms or sleeping areas <sup>[136](#o136)</sup>.

<a id="transport-to-work-lorry-rides"></a>

#### 3.12.6 Transport to work (lorry rides)

A lorry carrying workers in its rear deck must have:
- a canopy and side railings at least 700mm above the deck <sup>[137 (2024)](#o137)</sup>;
- rain covers on all open sides of the canopy (on all lorries since 1 January 2024) <sup>[137 (2024)](#o137)</sup>;
- the front passenger seats in the cabin filled before anyone rides in the back <sup>[137 (2024)](#o137)</sup>;
- at least 0.372 square metres (4 square feet) for each seated worker, with a Maximum Passenger Capacity label on the right of the tailboard showing how many may ride <sup>[137 (2024)](#o137)</sup>;
- a "vehicle buddy" sitting in the cabin beside the driver to keep the driver alert <sup>[137 (2024)](#o137)</sup>.

In the rear deck you must stay properly seated, keep every part of your body inside the lorry and not sit more than 1.1m above the deck <sup>[137 (2024)](#o137)</sup>. The driver should check that everyone is seated, with seat belts fastened where fitted, before moving off <sup>[137 (2024)](#o137)</sup>. If you are put in danger, such as an overcrowded lorry, tell your employer; if it refuses to act, report to the LTA (Land Transport Authority) on 1800-2255-582 <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>. Unsafe lorry transport can also be reported to MOM, the Traffic Police or the Migrant Workers' Centre on 6536 2692 <sup>[137 (2024)](#o137)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Must lorry rear decks now have seat belts for workers, and have any lorry-safety rules changed since MOM's November 2024 advisory update?

<a id="heat"></a>

#### 3.12.7 Heat

Employers must manage heat stress for outdoor work, using the WBGT (Wet Bulb Globe Temperature, a heat-stress index measured every hour; on-site meters are required at construction sites worth S$5 million or more, shipyards and the process industry) <sup>[138](#o138)</sup>:
- **New arrivals:** workers new to Singapore, or back from more than a week's leave, must be eased into the heat over at least 7 days <sup>[138](#o138)</sup>.
- **Water:** from WBGT 31°C, drink at least every hour, about 300ml an hour or more <sup>[138](#o138)</sup>.
- **Rest:** at WBGT 32°C or more, heavy outdoor work must stop for at least 10 minutes every hour; at 33°C or more, at least 15 minutes every hour, longer as it gets hotter <sup>[138](#o138)</sup>.
- **From 1 December 2026:** employers must also run heat-stress training, provide cool drinking water near the work area, keep cold water, ice packs or water spray ready for emergencies, and give clothing suited to the heat <sup>[139](#o139)</sup>.

Employers must report work-related heat illness to MOM <sup>[140](#o140)</sup>. Unsafe workplace conditions can be reported to MOM or to the Migrant Workers' Centre on 6536 2692 <sup>[137 (2024)](#o137)</sup>.

<a id="your-passport-and-work-permit-card"></a>

#### 3.12.8 Your passport and Work Permit card

Your passport is yours: employers should not keep workers' passports or force workers to hand them over, and should give you a safe place such as a locker that you can open at any time <sup>[141](#o141)</sup>. The employer must not keep your Work Permit card either <sup>[142](#o142)</sup>. Even so, MOM's 2024 survey found that over 31% of Work Permit holders did not hold their own passport <sup>[143](#o143)</sup>. If your employer refuses to return it when you ask, report it to MOM (6438 5122): in most cases employers returned passports promptly once MOM stepped in, and employers who refuse can be barred from hiring foreign workers <sup>[121](#o121), [143](#o143)</sup>.

<a id="mental-health-and-support"></a>

#### 3.12.9 Mental health and support

- **24-hour crisis helpline of HealthServe, a migrant workers' health charity:** +65 3129 5000, with staff and counsellors, some of whom speak workers' languages <sup>[N38 (2021)](#n38)</sup>; its tele-counselling in native languages runs Monday to Friday, 10am to 7pm <sup>[144 (2024)](#o144)</sup>.
- **Hear4U (Care Singapore), Bengali line:** 6978 2723, Monday to Friday <sup>[144 (2024)](#o144)</sup>.
- **Peer Support Leaders:** under MOM's Project DAWN, some dormitory residents are trained in psychological first aid to support others <sup>[N38 (2021)](#n38)</sup>.
- **FAST officers:** MOM's Forward Assurance and Support Teams, part of its ACE (Assurance, Care and Engagement) Group, visit dormitories to find and fix workers' problems; you can approach them in your dormitory <sup>[145](#o145), [121](#o121)</sup>.
- **HealthServe's clinic:** 1 Lorong 23 Geylang, near Aljunied MRT (metro) station, open 6.30pm to 9.30pm on Tuesdays, Wednesdays and Thursdays; Work Permit holders pay S$8 for an acute and S$15 for a chronic condition, and Special Pass holders (workers allowed to stay after their permit is cancelled, see [When the Work Permit is cancelled](#when-the-work-permit-is-cancelled)) pay nothing; call or WhatsApp +65 3157 4458 <sup>[N38 (2021)](#n38)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Are HealthServe's Geylang clinic hours (Tuesday to Thursday evenings) and fees (S$8 and S$15) still current in 2026?

<a id="your-work-permit"></a>

### 3.13 Your Work Permit

<a id="how-long-it-lasts"></a>

#### 3.13.1 How long it lasts

A Work Permit lasts up to 2 years, and can be shorter if your passport, the employer's security bond or your employment period ends sooner <sup>[48](#o48)</sup>. If your passport expires within 2 years, the permit lasts only until 1 month before it expires; to get the full 2 years, the passport should be renewed before the permit <sup>[146](#o146)</sup>. It is renewable <sup>[48](#o48)</sup>. A worker must be at least 18 and below 62 when the permit is applied for, and can keep working until age 64 <sup>[48](#o48), [7](#o7)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Is the maximum age at application "below 62" <sup>[48](#o48)</sup> or 62 <sup>[7](#o7)</sup>?

<a id="renewal"></a>

#### 3.13.2 Renewal

1. <a id="renewal-item-1"></a>**The employer renews it online,** 7 to 12 weeks before it expires, through MOM's WP Online system (the myMOM Portal from January 2027); the outcome is immediate <sup>[146](#o146), [147](#o147)</sup>. Your current employer has priority to renew you <sup>[12](#o12)</sup>.
2. <a id="renewal-item-2"></a>**The employer pays** the S$35 fee and the costs that come with renewal: the security bond, medical insurance and PCP <sup>[146](#o146), [131](#o131), [57](#o57)</sup>. Renewal, bond and insurance costs may never be taken from your salary <sup>[121](#o121), [124 (2024)](#o124)</sup>.
3. <a id="renewal-item-3"></a>**The employer gives you the notification letter.** It is valid for 1 month and lets you travel in and out of Singapore while you wait for the new card <sup>[146](#o146)</sup>.
4. <a id="renewal-item-4"></a>**Check it was done:** your new expiry date shows in the SGWorkPass app (see [The card and the SGWorkPass app](#the-card-and-the-sgworkpass-app)) <sup>[85](#o85)</sup>.

If you plan to travel shortly before the permit expires, the employer should renew it before you leave <sup>[146](#o146)</sup>. If it is not renewed and no other employer takes you on, the employer must send you home when the permit expires (see [Changing employer or leaving](#changing-employer-or-leaving)) <sup>[123 (2020)](#o123)</sup>.

<a id="the-card-and-the-sgworkpass-app"></a>

#### 3.13.3 The card and the SGWorkPass app

1. <a id="the-card-and-the-sgworkpass-app-item-1"></a>**The card.** After you register your fingerprints and photo, the card is delivered to the address given 4 working days later <sup>[48](#o48)</sup>. It is yours: the employer must not keep it <sup>[142](#o142)</sup>.
2. <a id="the-card-and-the-sgworkpass-app-item-2"></a>**The SGWorkPass app.** Download MOM's free SGWorkPass app (iOS, Android or Huawei HarmonyOS) and set up your digital work pass; you need Singpass (Singapore's government login), which first-time users register for, with help at a Singpass counter if needed <sup>[85](#o85)</sup>. The app works in Bengali <sup>[85](#o85)</sup>. It shows:
   - your pass and its validity, which you can show to any officer instead of the card <sup>[85](#o85), [6](#o6)</sup>;
   - the salary your employer declared to MOM <sup>[85](#o85)</sup>;
   - your employment history since January 2017 and your safety-course certificates <sup>[85](#o85)</sup>;
   - an Emergency or Get Help button that calls hotlines directly <sup>[85](#o85)</sup>.
   You can also check any Work Permit at www.mom.gov.sg/check-wp with the permit number and name <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>.
3. <a id="the-card-and-the-sgworkpass-app-item-3"></a>**"Invalid" in the app** means your pass has been cancelled or has expired, and you can no longer work <sup>[85](#o85)</sup>; ask your employer to check and fix the cause in WP Online <sup>[85](#o85)</sup>.
4. <a id="the-card-and-the-sgworkpass-app-item-4"></a>**If the card is lost, stolen or damaged,** the employer must apply for a replacement within 1 week, and a stolen card also needs a police report <sup>[148](#o148)</sup>. A lost or stolen card costs S$109 the first time and S$327 after that, a damaged one S$65.40 <sup>[148](#o148)</sup>. You collect a lost or stolen card's replacement in person at MOM Services Centre Hall C, 4 working days after approval, bringing your original passport, the card replacement letter, the completed declaration form and, for a stolen card, the police report <sup>[148](#o148)</sup>. If you lose it abroad, the employer must tell MOM at once, and MOM issues a letter for you to re-enter <sup>[148](#o148)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Who pays the S$109 card replacement fee when the worker lost the card: may the employer recover it from the worker?

<a id="going-home-on-leave-and-coming-back"></a>

#### 3.13.4 Going home on leave and coming back

1. <a id="going-home-on-leave-and-coming-back-item-1"></a>**Before you go:** have a valid passport, and carry it with your digital work pass or card, which you need to re-enter <sup>[149](#o149)</sup>. If you don't yet have the card, the digital pass or a valid notification letter is enough <sup>[150](#o150)</sup>.
2. <a id="going-home-on-leave-and-coming-back-item-2"></a>**Before you land back:** within 3 days before arrival, submit the free SG Arrival Card online or in the MyICA app (ICA is the Immigration & Checkpoints Authority) <sup>[117](#o117)</sup>; work pass holders are exempt only when crossing at the Woodlands and Tuas land checkpoints <sup>[117](#o117)</sup>.
3. <a id="going-home-on-leave-and-coming-back-item-3"></a>**Your employer's side:** it keeps paying the levy while you are away and can claim a waiver for leave of 7 days or more, up to 60 days a year <sup>[149](#o149)</sup>. Leave of over 3 months may need tax clearance <sup>[149](#o149)</sup>.
4. <a id="going-home-on-leave-and-coming-back-item-4"></a>**If you stay away long,** the employer may cancel your permit to stop the levy and would then need a new Work Permit to bring you back <sup>[149](#o149)</sup>. If you don't return, it cancels the permit; if you come back before its security bond is discharged, it must send you home <sup>[149](#o149)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Does any rule require the employer to pay the ticket for home leave, or is it only what the contract says?
> - How many days' home leave do Bangladeshi construction and shipyard workers usually get, and who usually pays the ticket?

<a id="when-the-work-permit-is-cancelled"></a>

#### 3.13.5 When the Work Permit is cancelled

The employer must cancel the permit within 1 week after your last day of notice, pay your ticket home and settle everything it owes you <sup>[6](#o6)</sup>. Once it is cancelled you cannot work, even while waiting to leave <sup>[33](#o33)</sup>. The employer prints a Special Pass (a short stay permit) valid until your departure date, which you show with your passport at the airport <sup>[33](#o33)</sup>. Leave before it expires, or you become an overstayer; if you need more time, the employer must ask for an extension before it expires <sup>[33](#o33)</sup>. After cancellation the card is cut in half and thrown away <sup>[33](#o33)</sup>; the full end-of-job steps are under Changing employer or leaving <sup>[33](#o33)</sup>.

<a id="if-you-leave-your-employer-without-notice-going-missing"></a>

#### 3.13.6 If you leave your employer without notice ("going missing")

If you disappear from your job, the employer must file a missing-person police report and cancel your Work Permit within 1 week <sup>[151](#o151)</sup>. Once it is cancelled you cannot work, and working for anyone else is illegal (see [What can get you jailed or deported](#what-can-get-you-jailed-or-deported)) <sup>[33](#o33), [152 (2024)](#o152)</sup>. If you are not found within 1 month, the employer loses S$2,500 of its S$5,000 security bond, a bond the employer pays for and must not charge to you; it should give you a copy of the bond's acknowledgement letter <sup>[151](#o151), [30](#o30)</sup>. If you have a salary claim or dispute with your employer, MOM tells you to approach MOM or TADM (the Tripartite Alliance for Dispute Management, which handles salary disputes) as early as possible (see [If something goes wrong](#if-something-goes-wrong)) <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>.

<a id="what-can-get-you-jailed-or-deported"></a>

### 3.14 What can get you jailed or deported

Breaking a Work Permit condition is itself an offence: it can bring a fine or jail, cancellation of the permit and a bar on ever working in Singapore again <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>. Breaking any other Singapore law can also lead to the permit being revoked and a ban on entering Singapore <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>. MOM cannot ban you from future work just because your employer complains about you; it can do so only if you are convicted of an offence or broke MOM's rules <sup>[151](#o151)</sup>.

<a id="overstaying"></a>

#### 3.14.1 Overstaying

Once your Work Permit or Special Pass ends, you must leave before it expires or you become an overstayer <sup>[33](#o33)</sup>. Overstaying up to 90 days is punishable by a fine of up to S$4,000, up to 6 months' jail, or both <sup>[36](#o36)</sup>. Overstaying more than 90 days brings up to 6 months' jail and at least 3 strokes of the cane; anyone who cannot be caned gets a fine of up to S$6,000 in place of the caning <sup>[36](#o36)</sup>.

<a id="working-for-someone-else-or-on-the-side"></a>

#### 3.14.2 Working for someone else or on the side

- **Only your employer, only your job.** You may not work for anyone other than the employer on your permit, in another job or sector, or in a business of your own <sup>[6](#o6)</sup>. This holds even if your own employer sends you to do it: report it to MOM on 6438 5122 <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>.
- **The penalty for the worker:** working without a valid work pass, or self-employed, is punishable by a fine of up to S$20,000, up to two years' jail, or both, and a bar on working in Singapore <sup>[152 (2024)](#o152), [153](#o153)</sup>. The employer who hires you illegally faces a fine of S$5,000 to S$30,000, up to a year's jail, or both <sup>[153](#o153)</sup>.
- **Finding jobs for friends.** Collecting money or papers from friends to get them jobs, or filing work pass applications for others, without an MOM licence is illegal: a fine of up to S$80,000 and/or up to 2 years' jail, the permit revoked and a ban <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>.

<a id="drugs-and-vaping"></a>

#### 3.14.3 Drugs and vaping

- **Drugs:** trafficking can mean up to 30 years' jail and 15 strokes of the cane, or death <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>. For cannabis, trafficking, importing or exporting more than 500 grammes carries the death penalty, and simply using it carries 1 to 10 years' jail and a fine of up to S$20,000 <sup>[154](#o154)</sup>.
- **Vapes (e-cigarettes):** vaping is illegal <sup>[155](#o155)</sup>. A foreigner caught with one has it seized and is issued a composition fine (a fine paid instead of prosecution) <sup>[155](#o155)</sup>, set at S$700 for an adult's first offence from 1 September 2025 <sup>[156](#o156)</sup>. Under the Tobacco and Vaporisers Control Act, in force since 1 May 2026, a court can fine an individual up to S$10,000 <sup>[155](#o155)</sup>. On a third offence a Work Permit holder may lose the permit and be deported and banned <sup>[155](#o155)</sup>.
- **Etomidate vapes ("Kpods"):** being caught with one, or testing positive for etomidate, can mean losing the permit, deportation and a ban at once; offences involving etomidate can bring a fine of up to S$20,000, up to 10 years' jail, or both <sup>[155](#o155)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Is the composition fine for an adult's first vape offence still S$700 under the Tobacco and Vaporisers Control Act since 1 May 2026?

<a id="alcohol-in-public-little-india-and-geylang"></a>

#### 3.14.4 Alcohol in public, Little India and Geylang

- **Everywhere:** no drinking in any public place from 10.30pm to 7am, and no take-away alcohol sales in those hours <sup>[157 (2015)](#o157)</sup>. Drinking at home and in licensed coffee shops, restaurants and bars within their hours is allowed <sup>[157 (2015)](#o157)</sup>.
- **Little India and Geylang (Liquor Control Zones):** public drinking is also banned all weekend, from 7am on Saturday to 7am on Monday, and from 7pm on the eve of a public holiday to 7am the day after it <sup>[157 (2015)](#o157)</sup>. For Deepavali 2026 the no-drinking period in these zones ran from 10.30pm on 6 November to 7am on 10 November <sup>[158](#o158)</sup>.
- **The penalty in a zone:** a fine of up to S$1,500; for a repeat offender up to S$3,000 and/or 4.5 months' jail <sup>[158](#o158)</sup>. A worker caught drinking in public in the banned hours has his Work Permit cancelled <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>.

<a id="littering-spitting-and-chewing-gum"></a>

#### 3.14.5 Littering, spitting and chewing gum

- **Littering:** fines of up to S$2,000 for a first conviction, S$4,000 for a second and S$10,000 after that, and the court can order up to 12 hours of cleaning public places <sup>[159](#o159)</sup>. NEA (the National Environment Agency) issued about 3,000 littering fines in the first three months of 2026 <sup>[159](#o159)</sup>.
- **Public behaviour:** MOM tells workers not to spit or litter, not to sleep in public places, and not to drink in large groups at the void decks (open ground floors) of housing blocks <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>.
- **Chewing gum:** bringing chewing gum into Singapore is prohibited, apart from health-related gum <sup>[160](#o160)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What is the current fine for spitting in a public place?

<a id="fights-riots-and-strikes"></a>

#### 3.14.6 Fights, riots and strikes

- **Gatherings:** taking part in an illegal assembly or procession, such as gathering in a large group in public to support or oppose anyone, is an offence and gets the Work Permit cancelled <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>.
- **Rioting:** caning and up to 7 years' jail <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>. After the Little India riot of 8 December 2013, Singapore deported 53 workers (52 Indian and 1 Bangladeshi) and charged 28 more; about 200 workers who were present but judged passive got police advisories and were allowed to stay <sup>[N39 (2013)](#n39)</sup>.
- **Strikes:** in November 2012, four SMRT bus drivers were charged in court for instigating an "illegal strike" <sup>[161 (2012)](#o161)</sup>; one was jailed for six weeks and 29 others were deported <sup>[N40 (2012)](#n40)</sup>. MOM tells workers with salary claims or disputes to approach MOM or TADM as early as possible (see [If something goes wrong](#if-something-goes-wrong)) <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>.

<a id="money-hundi-moneylenders-bank-accounts-and-sim-cards"></a>

#### 3.14.7 Money: hundi, moneylenders, bank accounts and SIM cards

- **Hundi or hawala:** running an unlicensed remittance business carries a fine of up to S$125,000, up to 3 years' jail, or both, and a worker who runs one on the side is also working without a valid pass (up to S$20,000, 2 years' jail, or both) <sup>[162 (2024)](#o162), [163](#o163)</sup>. A worker caught taking part has his permit revoked <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>. Use licensed remittance companies, which you can check at eservices.mas.gov.sg/fid (the Monetary Authority of Singapore's directory), and never send money through WhatsApp or other messaging apps <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>.
- **Moneylenders:** a worker found borrowing from an unlicensed moneylender has his permit revoked <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>. Acting as a runner or broker for one is punished by a fine of S$30,000 to S$300,000, up to 4 years' jail and up to 6 strokes of the cane <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>. Never lend your Work Permit to a friend to borrow money <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>.
- **Selling or lending your bank account (money mules):** in April 2026 the Police arrested 20 people, to be charged in court, who allegedly sold or handed over their bank accounts to scam syndicates <sup>[164](#o164)</sup>. Helping someone keep criminal money carries up to three years' jail, a fine, or both <sup>[164](#o164)</sup>; since 30 December 2025 a scam mule can also be sentenced to up to 12 strokes of the cane <sup>[164](#o164)</sup>. People involved in mule offences can be cut off from banking and mobile phone services <sup>[164](#o164)</sup>.
- **SIM cards:** handing a SIM card registered in your name to someone else, or letting others register SIM cards with your details, is an offence if you knew or had reason to believe they would be used for crime; you are treated as knowing if you did it for payment or without checking who would use them and why <sup>[165](#o165)</sup>. The penalty is a fine of up to S$10,000, up to three years' jail, or both <sup>[165](#o165)</sup>. Male CMP workers get a free Singapore SIM at the Onboard centre ([Step 7: Arrival and the Onboard centre](#step-7-arrival-and-the-onboard-centre)) <sup>[54](#o54)</sup>. To buy a prepaid SIM, the shop scans and uploads your passport or Work Permit and each registration gives one card, at most three prepaid cards per person; one of five people charged in 2022 was accused of keeping 37 people's passport or Work Permit numbers and registering 51 SIM cards in their names <sup>[N41 (2022)](#n41)</sup>.

<a id="bribery-and-false-claims"></a>

#### 3.14.8 Bribery and false claims

- **Bribery:** offering or taking a bribe is punished by up to 5 years' jail or a fine of up to S$100,000 or both <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>; report bribe demands to the CPIB (Corrupt Practices Investigation Bureau) 24-hour hotline, 1800-376-0000 <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>.
- **False injury claims:** a worker who makes a false work-injury claim is fined and/or jailed and cannot return to work in Singapore <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>.

<a id="marriage-and-pregnancy"></a>

#### 3.14.9 Marriage and pregnancy

- **Marriage:** a Work Permit holder, or someone whose last pass in Singapore was a Work Permit, must get MOM's approval before marrying a Singapore Citizen or Permanent Resident, in Singapore or abroad, in any form of marriage <sup>[166](#o166)</sup>. How to apply is under Family passes (see [Spouse of a Singapore citizen or PR](#spouse-of-a-singapore-citizen-or-pr)); MOM decides within 4 weeks of getting all the documents <sup>[166](#o166)</sup>. Marrying without approval can cost you the right to work in Singapore and bring a ban on entering <sup>[166](#o166)</sup>. The rule applies even after your permit has ended <sup>[166](#o166)</sup>.
- **Pregnancy:** a female Work Permit holder may become pregnant and give birth in Singapore only if she is already married, with MOM's approval, to a Singapore Citizen or Permanent Resident <sup>[166](#o166)</sup>.

<a id="bans-on-coming-back"></a>

#### 3.14.10 Bans on coming back

Working illegally, drug and vaping offences, unlicensed job-finding, unlicensed moneylending and breaking Work Permit conditions can all end with the permit revoked and a bar on returning to Singapore <sup>[121](#o121), [152 (2024)](#o152), [155](#o155)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How can a worker check whether he is barred from returning to Singapore before paying for a new job?

<a id="if-something-goes-wrong"></a>

### 3.15 If something goes wrong

<a id="if-you-arent-paid"></a>

#### 3.15.1 If you aren't paid

Salary is late if it is not in your account 7 days after the end of the month, and overtime if not paid within 14 days <sup>[100 (2024)](#o100), [122](#o122)</sup>. Come forward early: your chance of recovering everything is higher, you get time to find a new employer, and you can get help with food and housing if needed <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>. A salary claim must be filed within 1 year of the dispute if you are still employed, or within 6 months of your last day of work if you have left, and only salary owed in the 1 year before you file can be claimed <sup>[167](#o167), [168](#o168)</sup>. A claim for wrongful dismissal must be filed within 1 month of your last day <sup>[167](#o167)</sup>.

You'll need:
- your IPA letter, contract or KETs <sup>[169](#o169), [121](#o121)</sup>;
- payslips, bank statements and your time sheets or own record of hours <sup>[169](#o169), [121](#o121)</sup>;
- the termination or resignation letter, if any <sup>[169](#o169)</sup>;
- WhatsApp messages and emails with the employer <sup>[168](#o168)</sup>;
- the company's name, UEN (its business registration number), address and phone number <sup>[169](#o169)</sup>.

1. <a id="if-you-arent-paid-item-1"></a>**Get advice.** Call MOM on 6438 5122, or the Migrant Workers' Centre (MWC) 24-hour helpline on 6536 2692 <sup>[121](#o121), [N42 (2024)](#n42)</sup>. You can also use TADM's "Ask TADM" chatbot or book an appointment with a TADM advisory officer <sup>[167](#o167)</sup>. TADM, set up by MOM, the National Trades Union Congress and the Singapore National Employers Federation, handles salary and wrongful-dismissal claims; for work pass holders it sits at the MOM Services Centre, Hall A, Level 3, 1500 Bendemeer Road, Singapore 339946 <sup>[167](#o167), [170](#o170)</sup>. Its website can be read in Bengali <sup>[169](#o169)</sup>.
2. <a id="if-you-arent-paid-item-2"></a>**File the claim with TADM.** Online, you need Singpass, the documents above and a bank card or internet banking; it takes about 20 minutes <sup>[169](#o169)</sup>. The fee is S$10 for a claim of S$10,000 or less and S$20 above that, paid within 3 days (no cash; the kiosks at TADM take Visa or Mastercard, NETS debit and EZ-Link transit cards) or the claim is deleted <sup>[169](#o169)</sup>. You can claim up to S$20,000 per claim; anything above that must be given up <sup>[167](#o167)</sup>. TADM emails you the next steps within 3 working days of payment <sup>[169](#o169)</sup>. If the company is in bankruptcy, judicial management or liquidation, do not file at TADM: file with the Insolvency Office or the appointed judicial manager or liquidator <sup>[169](#o169)</sup>.
3. <a id="if-you-arent-paid-item-3"></a>**Expect your work pass to be cancelled, and get a Special Pass.** Employers typically cancel a claimant's work pass, and you are then given a Special Pass to stay while the claim runs <sup>[N43](#n43), [171](#o171)</sup>. Your employer must keep paying for your food, medical treatment and housing until the claim is resolved <sup>[123 (2020)](#o123)</sup>. Many employers resent workers who file claims and are slow to do this; if you need help with food and housing, go to MOM <sup>[N44](#n44), [121](#o121)</sup>.
4. <a id="if-you-arent-paid-item-4"></a>**e-Negotiation, then mediation.** For about a week you and the employer can make offers on TADM's online platform <sup>[168](#o168)</sup>. If that fails, a TADM mediator holds sessions of up to 3 hours, online or in person; mediation takes about 8 weeks <sup>[168](#o168)</sup>. Only you, the employer and the mediator attend: no lawyers, friends or family <sup>[168](#o168)</sup>. Attend every session: if you, the claimant, are absent, your case is discontinued <sup>[168](#o168)</sup>. A settlement is written into a settlement agreement; register it within 4 weeks online on CJTS (the Community Justice and Tribunals System, the State Courts' online filing system), for a S$10 fee, so it can be enforced like a court order <sup>[172 (2023)](#o172), [168](#o168)</sup>.
5. <a id="if-you-arent-paid-item-5"></a>**Look for a new job meanwhile.** Unless you choose to go home, TADM usually gives Work Permit holders a Change of Employer (COE) letter to find a new job while the claim runs <sup>[N43](#n43), [171](#o171)</sup>. The letter is valid for only two weeks, though some workers get it extended several times <sup>[N45](#n45), [N46](#n46)</sup>. Never work for anyone without a valid permit: claimants caught working illegally have their passes revoked and are barred from working in Singapore <sup>[171](#o171)</sup>.
6. <a id="if-you-arent-paid-item-6"></a>**If mediation fails, go to the Employment Claims Tribunals (ECT).** An unresolved claim is issued a claim referral certificate <sup>[167](#o167)</sup>. File at the ECT, part of the State Courts, within 4 weeks, online on CJTS, using Singpass or a CJTS Pass if you have no Singpass <sup>[173](#o173), [172 (2023)](#o172)</sup>. Have PDF copies of your passport and pass, the claim referral certificate, the employer's latest ACRA (company registry) profile and your supporting documents <sup>[173](#o173)</sup>. The fee is S$30 for a claim of S$10,000 or less and S$60 above <sup>[173](#o173)</sup>. While filing, pick a Case Management Conference (CMC) date, the earliest 3 to 4 weeks away, and the language you need an interpreter for <sup>[173](#o173)</sup>. Print the Notice of CMC and serve it on the employer within 7 days of filing, then file a declaration of service with proof, such as a registered post slip <sup>[173](#o173)</sup>. At the CMC a registrar tries to settle the dispute; if that fails, the case goes to a hearing before a tribunal magistrate <sup>[170](#o170)</sup>. Lawyers are not allowed <sup>[170](#o170)</sup>. Evidence must be in English or come with a certified translation, and interpretation into Bengali is free <sup>[172 (2023)](#o172)</sup>.
7. <a id="if-you-arent-paid-item-7"></a>**Get paid.** An ECT order can be enforced like a District Court order <sup>[172 (2023)](#o172)</sup>. If the employer goes bust or disappears, Work Permit holders may be paid from the S$5,000 security bond, but this works only for claims up to S$5,000 <sup>[N43](#n43), [30](#o30)</sup>.

In practice, in 2025, 88% of salary claims were settled at TADM mediation, 12% went to the ECT, and over 90% of claimants were fully paid; MOM restricts foreign hiring by employers who don't pay <sup>[174](#o174)</sup>. The whole process, from e-Negotiation to filing at the ECT, usually takes six months or less <sup>[N43](#n43)</sup>. Workers whose employers became insolvent or vanished often recovered nothing even after winning, and many felt pressured to accept less at mediation just to get some money and start working again <sup>[N43](#n43)</sup>. Workers with COE letters often end up paying unlicensed Bangladeshi job fixers to find a transfer job, some get the letter extended several times while others get only a short window, and employers can tell from the letter that the worker has filed a claim <sup>[N46](#n46)</sup>. In a 2025 case, a migrant worker got a Special Pass and a COE letter from TADM, and after three mediation sessions failed, the ECT ruled for him and he was paid in full <sup>[171](#o171)</sup>. In October 2026, about 230 workers, many of them Bangladeshi, of two process-sector companies, Process Maintenance Solutions and Jurong Technical & Construction, were owed up to two months' pay; MOM opened an investigation, and MWC fed about 90 of them, briefed them in Bengali and, with MOM, sped up their change of employer, while warning that the companies might not be able to pay <sup>[N47](#n47), [N48](#n48)</sup>. One of them, earning S$600 a month and repaying a S$9,000 bank loan taken to pay his agent, had his Work Permit, valid to July 2027, cancelled without a reason <sup>[N48](#n48)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How many times, and for how long each time, can TADM extend a COE letter?
> - Can a claimant without Singpass file a TADM claim in person at the MOM Services Centre, and what are the counter hours?

<a id="if-you-are-injured-at-work"></a>

#### 3.15.2 If you are injured at work

Under the Work Injury Compensation Act (WICA) you can claim, without a lawyer, medical leave wages, medical bills and a lump sum for permanent or current incapacity or death; you remain entitled even if your job ends or your work pass is cancelled <sup>[130](#o130), [121](#o121), [175](#o175)</sup>.

1. <a id="if-you-are-injured-at-work-item-1"></a>**Get treatment and tell your employer or supervisor at once,** or the employer may later dispute that the injury happened at work <sup>[175](#o175)</sup>. If the doctor gives you any medical leave or light duty, the employer must report the accident to MOM, and your claim then starts automatically: you do not file one yourself <sup>[175](#o175), [121](#o121)</sup>. You can also report the accident yourself through MOM's WSH (Workplace Safety and Health) Incident Reporting eService (iReport), so MOM can help if there is a dispute <sup>[175](#o175)</sup>. Once MOM has been notified, it posts you a letter with a case reference number for checking your claim's status <sup>[175](#o175)</sup>.
2. <a id="if-you-are-injured-at-work-item-2"></a>**Collect evidence.** Keep your WhatsApp and SMS messages about the accident, and ask a friend to photograph the place and the tools or machine that injured you <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>.
3. <a id="if-you-are-injured-at-work-item-3"></a>**Hand in your MCs and bills.** Give the original MC and medical bills to the employer and keep copies <sup>[175](#o175)</sup>. The employer must pay your medical leave wages and medical bills first and claim them back from its insurer later <sup>[175](#o175)</sup>.
4. <a id="if-you-are-injured-at-work-item-4"></a>**Check your pay.** Medical leave wages are due by your next pay day and are based on your average monthly earnings (AME) over the 12 months before the accident, usually including overtime <sup>[130](#o130)</sup>:
   - **Hospitalisation leave:** full AME for up to 60 days, then two-thirds from day 61 to one year <sup>[130](#o130)</sup>.
   - **Outpatient MC or light duty:** full AME for up to 14 days, then two-thirds up to one year <sup>[130](#o130)</sup>. On light duty the employer pays your actual wage plus any shortfall <sup>[130](#o130)</sup>.
   - **Not paid** for rest days and public holidays <sup>[130](#o130)</sup>.
   - **Medical bills:** the employer pays for treatment for up to 1 year or up to S$53,000 (accidents from 1 November 2025), whichever comes first <sup>[130](#o130)</sup>.
   - **Lump sum for permanent incapacity:** AME x an age factor x the % of permanent incapacity, which the treating doctor assesses once the injury stabilises; for accidents from 1 November 2025 it is at least S$116,000 and at most S$346,000, each multiplied by that % <sup>[130](#o130), [175](#o175)</sup>.
5. <a id="if-you-are-injured-at-work-item-5"></a>**If the employer doesn't report or pay,** alert MOM: call 6438 5122 or go to the Services Centre; you need only your contact details, the employer's name and the accident date, even if you speak no English <sup>[175](#o175), [121](#o121)</sup>.
6. <a id="if-you-are-injured-at-work-item-6"></a>**Attend every medical appointment.** Missing scheduled appointments suspends your claim <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>. Rest during medical leave; once you return to work, later medical leave can no longer be claimed <sup>[130](#o130)</sup>.
7. <a id="if-you-are-injured-at-work-item-7"></a>**Fill in the claim form.** The insurer posts you a claims processing form to confirm your earnings and injury <sup>[175](#o175)</sup>. Later it sends a notice of computation (NOC) with the amount; you can object within 14 days, otherwise payment is due within 21 days <sup>[175](#o175)</sup>. More than 75% of claims are settled within eight months of the accident <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>.

While the claim runs you stay on a Special Pass <sup>[176 (2024)](#o176)</sup>. The employer takes you to the MOM Services Centre and applies for it with a request letter, your passport, Work Permit card, medical appointment card and at least 3 photos of your housing <sup>[176 (2024)](#o176)</sup>. It promises to pay your food, housing, medical leave wages and bills, and not to send you home early without the authorities' consent <sup>[177](#o177)</sup>. You promise not to work, to attend all appointments, to live where MOM or the employer houses you, and to report to MOM on time to extend the pass, or you may be treated as a wilful overstayer <sup>[177](#o177)</sup>. Once you are off medical leave and a doctor's memo certifies you fit to work, you can ask MOM to let you work under the Temporary Job Scheme (TJS), which lets a Special Pass holder work temporarily for a new employer <sup>[178 (2021)](#o178)</sup>. Stay with your employer while the claim runs, as it must keep feeding and housing you; if it tries to send you home, call MOM on 6438 5122 <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>. If certified fit to travel, you may go home before the claim is settled, but tell MOM before you leave <sup>[179 (2024)](#o179)</sup>. Once the claim is settled, the employer must inform MOM's Transit Management Branch before buying your ticket home <sup>[177](#o177)</sup>.

Some people talk injured workers into withdrawing their WICA claim so that a lawyer can bring a common-law claim, for a commission; common-law claims take longer and need proof that the employer was negligent <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>. In a 2026 case, a Bangladeshi electrician who broke his foot was told by his boss that he would be sent home if he couldn't work; with the help of TWC2 volunteers he got his correct MC pay and lump sum <sup>[N44](#n44)</sup>.

<a id="if-you-are-abused-or-your-passport-is-kept"></a>

#### 3.15.3 If you are abused, or your passport is kept

- **Danger or violence:** call the Police on 999, or 1800 255 0000 <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>.
- **Passport or Work Permit card kept, salary cut, or work in another job:** report to MOM on 6438 5122 or at the MOM Services Centre, 1500 Bendemeer Road <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>. The employer must return your passport when you ask <sup>[141](#o141)</sup>.
- **Bad housing:** report to MOM <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>.
- **Kickbacks** (being made to pay to keep your job or renew your permit): report to MOM <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>.
- **If you must stay in Singapore to help an investigation:** the investigating agency gives you a Special Pass, and you are generally allowed to work under the Temporary Job Scheme (TJS); you don't apply: you are told if you are eligible, and a new employer applies to MOM for a TJS work permit to hire you <sup>[180](#o180)</sup>. MOM refers eligible Special Pass holders who need help finding a job to employment agencies, and helps those who can't pay for their upkeep <sup>[181 (2024)](#o181), [180](#o180)</sup>.
- **At any hour:** the MWC helpline 6536 2692, in about 10 languages, and HealthServe's crisis line +65 3129 5000 <sup>[N42 (2024)](#n42), [N38 (2021)](#n38)</sup>.

<a id="if-your-employer-tries-to-send-you-home-early"></a>

#### 3.15.4 If your employer tries to send you home early

The employer cannot force you home while it owes you salary or other money, and may not repatriate you in a way that defeats a salary or injury claim <sup>[121](#o121), [123 (2020)](#o123)</sup>. Some employers have used "repatriation agents" who escort workers to the airport <sup>[N23 (2014)](#n23)</sup>.

1. <a id="if-your-employer-tries-to-send-you-home-early-item-1"></a>**Before it happens:** if the employer is trying to send you home before your claim is settled, or stops your food or housing, ask MOM for help <sup>[175](#o175)</sup>.
2. <a id="if-your-employer-tries-to-send-you-home-early-item-2"></a>**If you are taken to the airport:** go to the Singapore Immigration (ICA) counter and say you have an unpaid claim; officers refer you to MOM, and ICA can issue a Special Pass so you can report to MOM <sup>[121](#o121), [N49 (2022)](#n49)</sup>.
3. <a id="if-your-employer-tries-to-send-you-home-early-item-3"></a>**File your claim** at TADM (see [If you aren't paid](#if-you-arent-paid)), or report the injury to MOM (see [If you are injured at work](#if-you-are-injured-at-work)) <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>.

Workers hearing this advice second-hand often fear that refusing to board will make them overstayers <sup>[N49 (2022)](#n49)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Do repatriation companies still escort workers to Changi Airport, and what do ICA officers do in practice when a worker refuses to board?

<a id="help-organisations"></a>

#### 3.15.5 Help organisations

- **MOM (Ministry of Manpower):** 6438 5122; MOM Services Centre, 1500 Bendemeer Road, Singapore 339946 <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>. FAST officers visit dormitories <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>.
- **Migrant Workers' Centre (MWC):** set up by the national trade union centre (NTUC) and the employers' federation (SNEF) <sup>[N6](#n6)</sup>. 24-hour helpline 6536 2692 in about 10 languages <sup>[N42 (2024)](#n42)</sup>; offices at 579 Serangoon Road (Singapore 218193) and #01-06 Atrix Building, 82 Geylang Lorong 23 (Singapore 388409); email feedback@mwc.org.sg <sup>[170](#o170)</sup>. Its Associate Membership gives critical illness, death and disability cover for S$6 in the first year <sup>[N6](#n6)</sup>. Its charity, the Migrant Workers' Assistance Fund (MWAF), gives distressed workers emergency shelter, daily essentials and help with salary arrears <sup>[N47](#n47)</sup>.
- **ACE (Assurance, Care and Engagement) Group:** MOM's division that protects migrant workers' employment rights and looks after their health care, housing and recreation; its FAST (Forward Assurance and Support Teams) officers work in dormitories <sup>[145](#o145), [121](#o121)</sup>.
- **TWC2 (Transient Workers Count Too):** 180B Bencoolen Street #09-01, The Bencoolen (office tower entrance), Singapore 189648; WhatsApp +65 6247 7001; office open 9.30am to 6pm Monday to Friday, but message first <sup>[N50](#n50)</sup>. Its Cuff Road Project in Farrer Park gives free meal cards to workers out of a job during a claim, used at 4 restaurants on weekday evenings and Saturday afternoons; it served 45,500 meals to 1,088 workers in 2025 <sup>[N44](#n44)</sup>.
- **HealthServe:** 24-hour crisis helpline +65 3129 5000; clinic at 1 Lorong 23 Geylang, free for Special Pass holders <sup>[N38 (2021)](#n38)</sup>.
- **ItsRainingRaincoats (IRR):** a charity offering free rides to the airport for Tuas residents flying home (Lakshmi, +65 9001 4936), a free store at 217 Henderson Road for male Work Permit holders (sign-up opens Sundays at 4pm), free dental and eye care, and luggage for home leave; contact it on Facebook <sup>[N51](#n51)</sup>.
- **Bangladesh High Commission, Labour and Welfare Wing:** Jit Poh Building, #04-00 and #10-00, 19 Keppel Road, Singapore 089058; High Commission main line (65) 6255 0075 <sup>[182 (2022)](#o182), [183 (2022)](#o183)</sup>. The head of the wing, the Minister (Labour): +65 6661 0298 (office), +65 8725 2452 (mobile), counselorsg@probashi.gov.bd <sup>[184](#o184), [185](#o185)</sup>. First Secretary (Labour): +65 6661 0277 (office), +65 9670 4080 (mobile), fssg@probashi.gov.bd <sup>[184](#o184), [185](#o185)</sup>. Applications are taken 9.30am to 1pm on weekdays <sup>[186 (2022)](#o186)</sup>. Its listed welfare services include visits to companies, hospitals, dormitories and prisons, representation in the courts, and dispute settlement <sup>[186 (2022)](#o186)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What does the Bangladesh High Commission's labour wing actually do for a worker with an unpaid claim or a forced repatriation (visit the employer, write to MOM, give a shelter)?
> - Is the First Secretary's mobile, +65 9670 4080 (Ministry list of May 2025), still in use, and does the wing run a hotline for workers?

<a id="changing-employer-or-leaving"></a>

### 3.16 Changing employer or leaving

<a id="if-you-want-to-change-employer"></a>

#### 3.16.1 If you want to change employer

You can move to a new employer without leaving Singapore only within your own sector, and only the new employer can apply <sup>[187](#o187), [12](#o12), [188](#o188)</sup>. Bangladesh is a Non-Traditional Source (NTS) country, and NTS workers are among those who can transfer <sup>[7](#o7), [12](#o12)</sup>. There are two ways:

- **With your current employer's consent, at any time** <sup>[12](#o12)</sup>:
  1. The new employer applies for a Work Permit for you; MOM sends your current employer an SMS or email <sup>[12](#o12)</sup>.
  2. Your current employer approves or rejects in the myMOM Portal within 7 days; if it does nothing, the application is rejected <sup>[12](#o12), [147](#o147)</sup>.
  3. Once approved, the new employer gets your new permit issued and the old one is cancelled automatically <sup>[12](#o12)</sup>.
  4. If your permit has less than 14 days left, the new employer must first get MOM's pre-approval, with a consent form signed by your current employer <sup>[12](#o12)</sup>.
- **Without consent, only in the window 40 to 21 days before your permit expires** <sup>[12](#o12)</sup>:
